#439. Where to watch Wild vs. Islanders today: Channel, live stream, time, TV schedule for Thursday NHL game

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Where to watch Wild vs. Islanders today: Channel, live stream, time, TV schedule for Thursday NHL game originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Minnesota Wild look to sustain their hot start as they welcome the New York Islanders on Thursday.

Minnesota (9-3-2) has the top record in the Western Conference so far and is second only to reigning champion Carolina in the league. The Wild are back at home after winning the last three games of a five-city road trip, most recently a 3-1 triumph at Utah that saw Kirill Kaprizov net his NHL-leading 14th goal.

The Isles (6-4-2) have looked better early on as Peter DeBoer begins his first full season as coach after the team missed the playoffs the last two years. New York blew a 3-0 lead in Nashville on Tuesday but managed to win in a shootout, a welcome bounceback after falling 6-3 in Dallas on Sunday.

Who will emerge on top Thursday? Here's all you need to know to tune in.

Live streams:Fubo (Twin Cities), DAZN (New York)

Tuesday's Wild vs. Islanders game will air locally on Wild+ in the Twin Cities area and MSGSN in the New York metro region.

Fans in the Islanders' broadcast territory can stream the game live with DAZN, which carries all MSG Network programming.

Wild vs. Islanders is set for Wednesday at 8 p.m. ET. The game will be played at Grand Casino Arena in St. Paul, Minnesota.

#462. “This Dude Can Cry About Everything”: Fans React As Lane Kiffin Complains About Schedule for Alabama Showdown

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Lane Kiffin has led the No. 15 LSU Tigers to a 6-2 start, including a 3-2 mark in SEC play, in his first year at the school. The Tigers host SEC rival Alabama, which is ranked No. 10, in their next game on Nov. 7 at Tiger Stadium in Baton Rouge.

Ahead of the game, Kiffin expressed his discontent over game time on Wednesday. Kiffin expected a night slot for a highly anticipated game like LSU-Alabama. However, the game between SEC titans, which has traditionally drawn a primetime spot in the past two decades, will be played at 2:30 p.m. CT.

Kiffin, who consistently bristled at LSU drawing night games when he was the coach at Ole Miss, decrying the advantage LSU derives from Tiger Stadium's mystique in night games, implied that the SEC doesn't want LSU to win, and people who speculate the opposite are wrong. Meanwhile, the SEC and LSU remain locked in contentious litigation, with the conference threatening to kick out the longtime member.

"My God this dude can cry about everything. If there was a conductor on a pity train, it would be Lane," one fan said.

LSU has played six of its eight games at night this season. The Alabama game has mostly been played at night in Baton Rouge since 2012. So, when the schedule came out, Lane Kiffin was unhappy.

"I was like, well, if someone thinks the SEC wants us to win this year after what's been going on, they must have just been on Mars the last four months," Kiffin said on Wednesday. "So, I guess when I look at it that way, I'm not surprised.

"Especially in 16 years, this game has been at night here all the time. I never even considered that it wouldn't be at night. So, I guess that's probably naive of me."

#595. Mbappe, Real Madrid and La Liga face long road to redemption in China

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kylian Mbappe, Real Madrid and La Liga have apologised for the superstar striker's comments about Mao Zedong, but the damage to their reputation in China won't be easily or quickly repaired, experts say.

The France captain, one of the best footballers on the planet, is at the centre of a major row after a video emerged this week of him appearing to make fun of former leader Mao.

Mbappe talked about being amused by an internet meme of his face photoshopped onto "some guy, think he's a Chinese dictator".

Mao remains officially revered as the hero of the communist revolution and founder of the People's Republic of China.

He is blamed by historians for millions of deaths, but publicly mocking him is taboo, and Mbappe's remarks saw him become the latest foreign sports figure or entity to infuriate Beijing.

Major Chinese brands cut ties with the 27-year-old, and Spanish top-flight matches will be pulled from state television broadcaster CCTV.

Chinese state media and social media users lined up to lambast Mbappe, although they were left in the dark about what exactly he said.

China has the world's second-biggest economy and can be hugely lucrative, and within two days the striker, his club Real Madrid, and La Liga all apologised.

A spokesman for China's foreign ministry said the Beijing government took Mbappe's apology "seriously".

"Viewed from a calm, objective and emotion-free standpoint, this is clearly a very damaging incident," Yan Qiang, a veteran sports commentator, told AFP.

"It causes serious damage to the image of La Liga and Real Madrid in China.

"In the age of the mobile internet things blow up on social media quickly and emotions run high.

"An apology does not necessarily mean the end of the story.

"It's better to have an apology than no apology at all, but it's definitely not the end of the story."

The Mbappe furore and fallout is not an unfamiliar tale.

Various overseas sports leagues or players have found themselves in hot water for comments on topics that are politically sensitive to China.

China effectively cut ties with the NBA in 2019 after then-Houston Rockets general manager Daryl Morey tweeted his support for Hong Kong protesters.

NBA executives said Morey had the right to freedom of expression. In contrast, La Liga immediately distanced itself from what Mbappe said.

The NBA, which along with football is the most popular sport in China, was wiped from CCTV for more than a year.

NBA pre-season matches only returned to China last year.

Australian National University lecturer Debby Chan called the NBA case -- and now Mbappe -- an illustration of China's consumer nationalism, where online sentiment and state media converge into "a spiral of outrage".

NBA commissioner Adam Silver said the league lost "hundreds of millions of dollars" over the split.

A source within La Liga told AFP that China and the United States alone finance the "majority" of international broadcasting rights, which were valued at 748 million euros ($834 million) for the 2024-25 season.

According to the Real Madrid-affiliated newspaper Marca, the Madrid club generates between 10 and 15 percent of its sales of jerseys and merchandise in China.

Real Madrid also counts Chinese companies among its main sponsors, with Hisense, sports betting firm Kaiyun Sports, and Linglong Tire all featuring prominently.

Adam Zhang, founder of the Beijing-based Key-Solution Sports Consulting, said showing fast and heartfelt remorse was the first step.

"This will influence the decision on whether to continue the broadcast ban and, if so, for how long," he told AFP.

Sports commentator Yan cast doubt on whether the reputation of Mbappe, whose face has adorned drinks bottles and advertising campaigns in China, can ever fully recover.

"Judging by commercial sponsorships and big ad campaigns he appeared in during the World Cup, he has a huge influence in China," he said.

"If that influence can't be sustained there can be no talk of commercial interests, and La Liga and Real Madrid will definitely be badly affected."

Yan said it was "genuinely hard to predict" what happens next.

"In the short term, I'm certainly not optimistic," he added.

"If they want to repair their image and the relationship, their future actions and behaviour will be key."

#28. Milwaukee Brewers defeat Tampa Bay Rays to claim first World Series title

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Milwaukee Brewers’ 57-season wait for a World Series title ended Wednesday as their victory over Tampa Bay completed one of the most dominant postseason runs in MLB history.

Once closer Trevor Megill induced a soft ground ball for the last out and their 4-1 win over the Rays in Game 5 was final, the Brewers rushed up the steps of their dugout and onto the playing field at Tropicana Field in St. Petersburg, Florida. The celebration had been in the making for more than half a century.

“This is one of the greatest moments of my life,” Brewers shortstop Cooper Pratt said on Fox after the game. “Oh my gosh. It doesn’t even feel real. This is surreal.”

After having relocated from Seattle to Milwaukee and played for the first time as the Brewers in 1970, the team didn’t make the World Series until 1982, when it lost in seven games. It then waited 44 years to make its return to the Fall Classic.

For a franchise starved for big moments, this year’s team supplied one after another.

The Brewers’ 12-2 postseason record is among the best since the MLB postseason format expanded in 1995 to include wild-card teams. The Brewers’ .857 winning percentage was just off the percentages produced by the 1999 New York Yankees and the 2005 Chicago White Sox, which each went 11-1 en route to their own titles.

“I don’t even have words,” star pitcher Jacob Misiorowski, who threw 5 2/3 scoreless innings of the clinching game, told Fox after the Game 5 win.

Milwaukee is MLB’s smallest market. Its payroll ranked only 19th out of 30 teams. In 12 postseason games, it trailed nine times. But the Brewers won seven of those games by finding ways to prevail in multiple ways. Milwaukee hit seven home runs in a National League Championship Series sweep of the Los Angeles Dodgers yet took a 3-0 series lead on Tampa Bay while hitting just one homer. In Game 5, Milwaukee drove in runs using two singles, a sacrifice fly and a double.

Down to its final out in the bottom of the ninth inning, Tampa Bay got on the board when Chandler Simpson’s two-out double drove in Liam Hicks to trim the deficit to 4-1. With hard-throwing reliever Aaron Ashby waiting to relieve Megill in case he got into any more trouble, a soft grounder ended the game.

Catcher William Contreras earned Most Valuable Player honors after driving in four runs and hitting .381 during the World Series.

“I don’t have words to say,” Contreras said through a translator.

This was no fluke by a scrappy team that got hot at the right time. Milwaukee had the second-best record in baseball at July’s All-Star break and ultimately won an MLB-high and franchise-record 103 games. After it beat San Diego in the divisional round, it swept two-time reigning champion Los Angeles in the National League Championship Series. Even though the Dodgers’ payroll was more than double the Brewers’, Milwaukee scored 10 more runs over the sweep to secure a World Series berth against Tampa Bay.

The Rays were in some ways a mirror image of Milwaukee, another light-spending franchise looking to turn its regular-season success — the Rays had won the most games in the American League — into its first World Series victory.

“Best feeling ever,” Christian Yelich, the Brewers’ captain, said on the broadcast.

After one-run wins in the first two games of the World Series — including a Game 2 comeback in which Milwaukee fell behind 4-0 in the first inning — it won Game 3 in a 10-1 rout sparked by Christian Yelich’s stolen base in the fifth inning. When a challenge ruled Yelich safe at second base, Milwaukee proceeded to score eight runs with two outs, blowing the game open without ever hitting a home run.

Milwaukee’s championship run was possible thanks to a dependable core of players who spanned generations. Yelich, 34, and 33-year-old reliever Brandon Woodruff, whose save in Game 3 was his first appearance since July 4 because of injuries, were the only players remaining from a 2018 Brewers roster that was just one win away from that season’s World Series.

In the divisional round against San Diego, 20-year-old outfielder Jackson Chourio hit a late two-run homer that saved Milwaukee in its opening game of the postseason. Chourio then delivered a walk-off single to win the following game.

On the mound, Milwaukee constructed a feared rotation of arms starting with Misiorowski, the 24-year-old with blazing speed who made the All-Star Game as a rookie in 2025 after just five starts. For his encore, Misiorowski in 2026 led a pitching staff that allowed MLB’s second-lowest team ERA during the regular season. When he left games, a bullpen with arms just as nasty and hard-throwing as Misiorowski’s took over.

“This is just incredible,” Megill, on the mound for the final out, said after the win. “Ten-year-old me would be so proud.”

All of the pieces worked, all season. When the regular season ended, Milwaukee stood alone at the top of the standings.

And when the playoffs ended a month later, the Brewers were on top yet again. Their long wait for a title had ended.

Andrew Greif is a sports reporter for NBC News Digital.

#192. Billionaire Ambani cries tears of joy as Jio Platforms becomes the second most valued Indian firm

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Billionaire Mukesh Ambani has become the owner of the two most valued companies in India, after a stellar listing of Jio Platforms, the country's largest telecom operator and digital services firm.

The share of Jio Platforms continued to climb higher on Thursday, opening 3.3% higher a day after its market debut.

The Meta- and Google-backed company listed on Wednesday at a premium of over 25% to its IPO price, leading to a market capitalization of 13.05 trillion rupees ($137.1 billion).

Ambani's flagship company, Reliance Industries, also the most valued company in India, owns more than a 64% stake in Jio Platforms after the initial public offering.

Ahead of the listing, the patriarch of the Ambani family teared up as his three children shared the journey of building Jio and vowed to create value for investors over the next 50 years.

Analysts expect Jio's free cash flow to improve significantly as the funds from the initial public offering will be used to repay legacy telecom debt, and the company's 5G investment cycle is slowing down.

However, they cautioned that most of the value has been priced in after the listing gains, with the company now being valued on par with some of the global telecom giants.

With just 3% share dilution, Jio has wiped out nearly 300 billion rupees of debt, said Anurag Singh, founder and managing partner of U.S. based Ansid Capital, told CNBC. He added Jio's current market cap puts it in the league of American telecom giants such as AT&T and Verizon Communications.

AT&T has a market cap of $139 billion and Verizon of $179 billion, as per LSEG data.

In the next 10 years, Jio will "democratize every technology that can transform Indian lives," Akash Ambani, the eldest son of Mukesh and the managing director of Jio Platforms, said on Wednesday, ahead of the listing.

The 35-year-old scion listed out artificial intelligence, Cloud, connected devices, and "connectivity from even space," as some of the key areas for future growth.

The company's plans to launch satellite communications drew the ire of trillionaire Elon Musk, who blamed the Ambanis for the delays in the launch of Starlink in India earlier this month.

"Jio brings a rare scale to Indian equities," with a customer base of more than half a billion, operating margins of 52% and net leverage of 0.36 times, Vaqarjaved Khan, senior analyst at Indian broking firm Angel One, told CNBC.

He added that at a price-to-earnings of 34 times, much of the "quality" is priced in and future returns will be driven by improvement in average revenue per user and AI services.

Meanwhile, after Jio's listing pop, Reliance Group's market value of 30 trillion rupees has surpassed that of Tata Group's 25 trillion rupees, making Ambani family business the top conglomerate in the country, as per a Bloomberg report.

#103. Google has a plan to keep ads alive even when AI agents do the shopping

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google has a pitch for advertisers worried AI agents will make Search obsolete: Let the bots do the shopping legwork, but leave people to compare brands, prices, perks — and sponsored offers.

"When users shop, and that's important, there's a part of the journey they don't like, the grunt work, which is frankly fantastic to automate with AI," Philipp Schindler, Google's chief business officer, said on Alphabet's earnings call on Tuesday. "But we also want to enhance, not just automate away the discovery experience that people frankly love in so many cases."He said users do not always want the cheapest option; they also weigh brand loyalty and customer service. Google believes that this decision-making process gives it space to build ads into agentic shopping.

He shared an example of how that might work: "Here's the insurance product we found, and here's another strong option, one of which is a higher premium, maybe a lower deductible, and they are currently offering a very special discount."

Schindler added that there is almost never a single perfect solution for any shopping decision, which makes Google "confident" that there is room for advertising even in agentic search.

The comments offer a clearer explanation of Google's business model for agentic search, which has presented a challenge for its core ads business. On Tuesday, the company said that Google's AI mode has led to more searches than ever, but it's not making money at the same rate. It's a major concern among investors, who have long argued that AI chatbots and agents will threaten Google's search business.

Google has been signaling for months that it wants to solve that problem without simply cramming old-style ads into a chatbot. Last quarter, Google said it was testing ideas like showing offers during travel planning and clearly marked sponsored links inside AI-generated lists.

Alphabet reported third-quarter revenue of $127.58 billion, narrowly below the $127.97 billion analysts expected. Earnings per share came in at $2.12, versus roughly $3.01 expected, largely because of an $8.2 billion unrealized loss on its SpaceX investment. Shares fell about 10% after hours.

On Wednesday night’s episode of “The Tonight Show,” host Jimmy Fallon went full Nathan Fielder while guest Jamie Lee Curtis played Elizabeth Holmes to spoof A24’s buzzy documentary, “You Can See Everything.”

I love talking to you,” responded Curtis with her best Holmes-like voice while donning a blonde wig and looking deeply into the late-night host’s eyes. “So, you’re not just here to promote your new show. ‘Newlyweds’?” “No, I’m engaging with you as a human being,” said Curtis. “Why would I deceive you? There’s no reason for me to do that. “Good chat,” concluded Fallon, but not before one last question: “You’re being real right now?” “I’m always being real,” responded Curtis. As Fallon leaves, she yells, “Thank God! Fuck, what an asshole!”

#222. What we know so far about deadly Spain train crash

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: Aerial footage shows train crash scene

Two trains have crashed in Spain just south of the capital Madrid on Wednesday morning.

Rail operator Adif says the incident occurred between a Renfe train and another of the Iryo company, which covers the Madrid to Seville route.

So far at least 68 people are confirmed to have died in the incident.

Here is what we know about the crash so far.

The two trains crashed just after 09:00 (08:00 GMT) between municipalities Mora and Orgaz, and about 140km (100 miles) south of Madrid.

The accident happened when a high-speed train travelling from Madrid to the south of the country barrelled into another train that was apparently stationary. The crash caused at least two carriages to fall down an embankment.

Broadcaster RTVE, quoting the transport ministry, said a Renfe train rear-ended an Iryo train.

Renfe is Spain's national state-owned railway company while Iryo is a private high-speed railway operator.

All train traffic on this line has been stopped at stations along the route, Adif said.

Renfe said services between Madrid, Ciudad Real and Andalusia remain suspended.

A passenger on board one of the trains told local media there was a heavy impact about 40 minutes into the journey.

Iryo said its train was "struck from behind" by a Renfe train "due to causes that are still unknown.

Transport minister Oscar Puente has said a computer failure led to the initial halt of six moving trains, including the two trains that subsequently collided.

"In the event of a system failure, the control centre takes over," he said, adding it was the control centre which decided to allow the trains to move again, without any speed restrictions.

That decision, Puente said, was made "under inexplicable circumstances". It resulted in the Renfe train crashing into the Iryo train, which was stationary on the tracks further ahead.

Audio recordings between train controllers in Madrid and the train drivers appear to show that one high-speed train was given clearance to move even though another up ahead had stalled.

Bloodied, dazed and angry - how passengers' rail journey turned to disaster in Spain

High-speed train crash kills at least 68 people in Spain

Aerial footage shows the extent of the damage and the emergency response

More than 500 people were on board both trains.

Esther Padilla, spokesperson for the government of Castilla-La Mancha region, told TVE that one of the trains - managed by the Iryo company - was carrying 146 passengers plus one baby, while the Renfe-managed train had 397 passengers on board.

A former government minister was also among those reported missing.

The Spanish Confederation of Business Organizations (CEOE) posted on X that, FÃ¡tima BÃ¡Ã±ez - who heads the organization's foundation - was on board one of the trains involved in the crash.

Eighty people are being treated for injuries, according to Spanish authorities, with 50 of the casualties said to be in a serious condition, and 15 more in a critical condition.

Spanish Prime Minister Pedro SÃ¡nchez has said "the whole of Spain is grieving" and that "lives have been shattered, families have been torn apart".

"The pain is immense," he said on a visit to the crash site.

The government was "trying to determine what was the origin of this tragedy, of this terrible accident," he added.

"We will get to the bottom of this, and if there has been any negligence, it will come to light.

"If there have been failures in the security system, they will be addressed with the utmost firmness."

SÃ¡nchez and other Spanish politicians have cancelled all public engagements on Wednesday, including campaigning for next month's parliamentary elections.

Three days of official mourning began on Thursday.

Spain's royal family posted on X that it was "shocked and concerned" and expressed condolences for the relatives of the deceased and those affected by the crash.

A spokesperson for the EU said the bloc was ready to help Spain if needed.

A huge emergency response was under way with fire crews and helicopters at the scene.

Hospitals in the area have suspended planned surgeries as they deal with an influx of patients from the crash, Spanish media have reported.

A blood transfusion centre in Madrid has also appealed for "urgent" donations. In a post on X, Centro de TransfusiÃ³n said it made the request "in the face of a possible sudden increase in care needs".

"The top priority is the identification of the missing persons and the taking of DNA samples to be able to locate their relatives," the civil guard wrote in a post on X.

This is the second high-speed rail tragedy in Spain this year.

In January a high-speed train derailed in southern Spain, its rear carriages going into the path of a state-run Renfe train, claiming the lives of 46 people.

#111. Shopify CEO turned his wine cellar into an AI rack — and uses it to find his most stressful meetings

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Shopify CEO didn't have enough wine to fill his cellar, but he had plenty of uses for an AI system.

Speaking with Alex Heath on an episode of the Sources podcast released on Wednesday, Tobi Lütke said he consulted his “inner 16-year-old” before converting the underused room into a home for computing hardware.

"So now I have a rack cellar, and it has like one of those GB300s from Dell in it," Lütke said. "So it is actually fully training models."

The GB300 refers to Nvidia's Grace Blackwell Ultra computing platform, which Dell has built into powerful computers designed to develop and run AI models.

Lütke said the setup allows him to run AI locally, working with sensitive information he can't send to an outside lab. The agent researches topics, prepares briefings, and gathers data about his daily life, he said.

“I think it's probably the most compelling product I've ever encountered in my life," Lütke said. "It runs a script that sort of makes a collection of all sorts of things, including my Fitbit data, and, you know, obviously has my calendar."

"It has a little app. It knows my geolocation," Lütke added. "For my hobby, I do a lot of health-style analysis, but then given that I have all this data, like I have heart rate data for, you know, for this, and I figured out who my most stressful meetings are with."

Tech bosses are finding increasingly personal uses for AI. Business Insider previously reported that Google CEO Sundar Pichai uses agents to follow internal discussions and computing usage. Apple CEO Tim Cook uses AI to summarize emails, while OpenAI's Sam Altman has turned to ChatGPT for parenting questions.

Lütke has also pushed employees to put AI to work. In a memo made public in April 2025, he said that all Shopify teams must demonstrate why they can't achieve their goals with AI before hiring new employees.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

Asked by Heath whether he told the people he discovered were stressing him out, Lütke said: “Yeah. Totally.”

"It's like: here's the funniest thing I just find out — guess what?" Lütke said. "And then they didn't think it's nearly as funny."

#357. Bluesky Is Blocking Links to a Major Leak of DHS Employee Records

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bluesky is blocking users from opening links to a newly leaked archive of records on more than 41,000 Department of Homeland Security employees and contractors, and labels the pages “malicious.”

The files include people’s email addresses and phone numbers, “shared without their authorization,” Bluesky tells WIRED, in violation of its community guidelines. The company says it chose to block the links, rather than remove individual posts, “to avoid limiting public discussion or restricting broader access to reporting and commentary.”

The DHS records, published Wednesday by the transparency nonprofit Distributed Denial of Secrets, list employees' and contractors’ names, contact information, and membership in internal groups, tied in some cases to surveillance and undercover operations. A WIRED investigation found that the files lay out in detail how DHS is organized, from senior leadership to individual immigration agents, and in some cases appear to show who has access to certain technologies.

Users who try to open the archive through Bluesky’s website or app land on a page labeled "Blocked Link," which says the URL has been identified as “malicious” and blocked for their safety. Bluesky did not respond to questions about whether the block was imposed automatically or whether any government agency had asked the company to restrict access to the data.

The DDoSecrets homepage loads normally from Bluesky. At least one third-party app, Klearsky, opens links to the DHS archive with no warning.

Bluesky promotes a decentralized approach to content moderation that lets users pick independent services that label or filter posts. Its current community guidelines bar sharing “home addresses, personal contact info, financial or medical data, or identification documents.” (A preview of the DHS leak analyzed by WIRED apparently contained work-related contact information one expert described as details that would be printed “on a business card.”) The same guidelines explicitly protect “factual reporting” on matters of public interest that are “related to government transparency and public officials.”

Bluesky started in 2019 as a Twitter research project, later became independent, and opened to the public in February 2024. By August of this year, it counted 45 million registered accounts, and an estimated 10.4 million people used its mobile app in June. Toni Schneider, the founding CEO of Automattic, has run the company since cofounder Jay Graber stepped down in March. Because the network runs on an open protocol, outside developers can build their own Bluesky apps and decide which of the company’s moderation labels to apply.

Emma Best, cofounder of DDoSecrets, says this is not the first time a social media platform has wrongly labeled the organization's website malicious or tried to restrict access to its publications. Twitter suspended DDoSecrets’ account in 2020, prior to the Elon Musk acquisition and X rebrand, and blocked links to its website after the group published BlueLeaks, a 269-gigabyte trove of internal records from hundreds of police agencies and fusion centers. Best says tech platforms often give in to government pressure but insists they cannot prevent the information from spreading. “They can't stop the signal,” they say.

A group calling itself the Department of Peace gave the archive, dubbed “Icepick,” to DDoSecrets and journalist maia arson crimew. crimew also built a website for searching the records, and Bluesky blocks that site in its website and app as well.

At least one of Bluesky’s competitors has taken a different tack. On X, the Grok chatbot built by X’s parent company pointed curious users to the Icepick website, describing it as a “cleaned searchable web tool” and saying that the complete dataset can be downloaded via DDoSecrets’ website or by torrent. X did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

#344. Claude Code burns ~15–20% of your tokens on a 3-word status line

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Instantly share code, notes, and snippets.

Tested on Linux, Claude Code 2.1.257–2.1.312 (see Tested versions). The binary is not modified (BUN_OPTIONS=--preload). An update can silently undo it, so see Verify.

To make it permanent for every Claude Code process, see Install.

Every ~30 seconds, for every background agent, Claude Code sends that agent's entire transcript to the agent's own model (usually Opus) and asks it to "describe your most recent action in 3-5 words". The answer only updates the agent's status line in the agents panel; it never reaches the model or your conversation. There is no setting that turns it off, and since 2.1.232 (August 2026) it has been on by default.

The anomaly was detected by DRIM in Claude Code's OpenTelemetry export: grouping claude_code.api_request events by query_source showed agent_summary costing more than the main conversation itself.

Spend here means Claude Code's own per-request estimate (cost_usd), not an invoice: on a subscription, what you lose is rate-limit headroom. We are not the only ones who measured it: #86879 collects four independent measurements of 18–26 %.

no-agent-summary.js is a Bun preload (~80 lines of code). It wraps setTimeout/setInterval; when a callback's source text contains a summarizer anchor (e.g. the [AgentSummary] Timer fired log line), it returns an inert handle instead of arming the timer. Every other timer passes through untouched. In the OpenTelemetry export the suppressed requests are claude_code.api_request events with query_source = agent_summary.

Why a timer hook rather than something simpler: the module ships as precompiled Bun bytecode, so editing its JS text inside the binary changes nothing, and the API client does not go through a wrapped globalThis.fetch. But Function.prototype.toString still returns a callback's source even when it runs from bytecode, so the timer can be recognised by a string inside it. The inert timer uses exactly 2³¹−1 ms: Node and Bun treat a larger delay as 1 ms, so an "infinite" delay would fire at once.

Put no-agent-summary.js somewhere stable; note its absolute path.

Set BUN_OPTIONS globally so every Claude Code process gets it. One of:

Or a wrapper named claude earlier on PATH (the case keeps it idempotent when one Claude Code process starts another):

Restart the daemon and every already-running session, then start Claude Code from an environment that has BUN_OPTIONS:

Expect preload active mode=off …. No line: BUN_OPTIONS did not reach the process, or the path is not absolute.

Timer suppressed: with the same BUN_OPTIONS and CC_AGENT_SUMMARY_LOG=/tmp/nas.log, run a background subagent for a minute or two. The log shows summary timer #1 suppressed (setTimeout, delay=30000) and the agent's panel status stops updating. If you export OpenTelemetry, query_source = agent_summary should stay at zero; this is the only check that catches every failure mode.

Matching is by source text: an anchor must sit inside the callback passed to the timer. If an update breaks that, the hook matches nothing and summaries silently come back. Ways it can happen:

Linux x64 binary from npm @anthropic-ai/claude-code-linux-x64 (its sha256 equals the native installer's manifest.json checksum). Static: the extracted bundle's summary timer callback carries a hook anchor. Live: claude -p --model haiku with CLAUDE_CODE_AGENT_PROGRESS_SUMMARIES=1, one subagent doing 8 steps of ~20 s, counting [AgentSummary] Forking in --debug-file.

5 / 5 / 0 = the fork happens on this version, merely loading the hook changes nothing, and off removes it.

In our telemetry its weekly share of spend went from 3.5–7 % in July (the fork gate was already open for us) to 7.3 % and then 11.8 % in the week we picked up 2.1.233, and to about 16 % by mid-October.

#379. ‘South Park’: Cartman, Stan and Kyle Must Solve South America’s Beef Crisis in Halloween Week Episode

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the fourth episode of “South Park” Season 29, South America’s beef has surprisingly become a hot commodity — and Cartman, Stan, Kyle and Randy won’t let their lunch-of-choice get taken away from the local mountain town without a fight.

The episode, titled, fittingly, “Guinea Pig on a Stick,” opens with Cartman eager to get a sloppy joe for lunch at South America Elementary. But to his horror, he learns there’s a national beef crisis, so the government is taking it from South America, and he and the gang will have to eat different versions of the four-legged rodent instead. But the kids (and Randy, who remains shrunken) won’t stand for this, and set off to find a solution after reflecting on what Kenny (who got blown up by an e-bike in the first episode of Season 29) would have done: not stand for this. Related Stories 'KPop Demon Hunters,' 'Squid Game' Stars Fete Netflix's Korea Decade as Ted Sarandos Teases 'Tantara,' 'All of Us Are Dead' Season 2 ‘KPop Demon Hunters’ Gets New Tech Collection at Target, Including Ramen-Themed Chargers, Soda Can Speaker, Pat Pat Light and More

They visit the local ranch, where they learn that the suddenly premier, in-demand South American beef is just “the same cows that have always been here.” But, as the ranch worker responds, “Now we have a much higher clientele, you know, and all the rich people in other states want a premium experience.” He’s referring to national clientele outside of South America — specifically the chain Ruth’s Chris Steak House — despite the fact that every person who indulges in the meat literally explodes with diarrhea.

Those eating the beef don’t care, though, as it’s become a symbol of “who’s more patriotic,” explains Randy, with politicians wanting to prove they support local South American beef. Plus, in the end, another chain, Sizzler, swoops in and steals all the cows. With no beef left, Stan has an epiphany: “They said nobody even really cares about our cows… So then, this rancher could inject anything with lard and butter, and people would be just as obsessed with it. That’s it, dude. That’s it. We can have all our cows back.” Indeed, it works, as the episode ends back at Ruth’s Chris Steak House, where a waiter is serving diners their new “patriotic meals”: flame-seared guinea pig on a stick.

This week’s episode was decidedly not a Halloween special, despite the show’s storied history of spooky holiday episodes and its airing just a few days earlier. Thus, it appears creators Trey Parker and Matt Stone are skipping the holiday altogether. And though last year’s episode, titled “The Woman in the Hat,” wasn’t advertised as a Halloween episode ahead of its air date, it did come two days later than normal, timed to fall on the spooky night of Oct. 31.

So far, Season 29 has centered a range of much-talked-about topics, including e-bikes, data centers, billionaires and anti-vaxxeres. In the third episode, the crew found themselves stuck in Sephora’s “backroom,” a nod to the joke that tweens spend ages in the beauty store, and dove into the internet-era term aura battles (meaning lingo like “mogging,” “maxxing” and “mewing” were plentiful).

With Season 29, creators Trey Parker and Matt Stone are going full steam ahead with the “South America” name change, which was announced earlier this month ahead of the Season 29 premiere. The premiere — the first episode since taking on the name “South America” — hit 4.2 million viewers in three days, as previously reported by Variety, and even featured an updated theme song to ring in the name change (and a Donald Trump cameo).

#248. Anduril now says its BlueChip laptop will meet Buy American standards at launch

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Palmer Luckey says it was always the goal, and now the company is committing to it.

Palmer Luckey says it was always the goal, and now the company is committing to it.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

On Tuesday, defense contractor Anduril and chipmaker Qualcomm announced a partnership to build laptops entirely in the United States, starting with a single model in 2027. They also announced a “long-term goal” of pushing the computer industry to create “a fully domestic supply chain.”

Even a single Made-in-USA laptop would be a surprise, because no major US computer company builds its laptops here — not Apple, not Dell, not HP, not Microsoft. We’re not aware of any company that does.

But we are aware of companies that have briefly and strategically assembled computers here seemingly to appease President Donald Trump. We’re also aware of companies that have promised US jobs only to deliver empty buildings. And we’re aware of at least one fake “US” company that lied to the government about making product domestically.

That’s why we now ask pointed questions when a company says it’s committed to US manufacturing. We asked TP-Link those sorts of questions just over a week ago. On Monday, we asked Anduril founder Palmer Luckey and Qualcomm president and CEO Cristiano Amon those kinds of questions.

Over the course of two days, Anduril has changed one important answer.

Anduril communications VP Shannon Prior now says the company’s laptop will have at least 65 percent of its components made in the US by the time it launches in 2027. It’s an important number, and one that Anduril did not commit to earlier this week.

Luckey held a call with reporters on Monday, during which he was clear that he wants to make literally every part of a computer in the United States — and that Anduril and Qualcomm have already mapped out a plan. “We have a technical roadmap to get to 100 percent domestic over time,” he told journalists.

Luckey also suggested his laptop would be built to comply with the requirements of the military and other government agencies — and that it would meet or exceed one specific US government standard that has an exact definition.

“We are not just barely meeting the bar for Made in America. We are going hard to make this an all-American play, and not even the Buy American Act requires what we’re trying,” Luckey said.

The Buy American Act dates back to 1933, but it was updated during the Biden Administration to require that at least 65 percent of the cost of a company’s products must be made domestically for it be considered a domestic product itself. That number is going up to 75 percent in 2029.

Many products need to be made in America to those standards, or federal agencies can’t buy them without receiving a waiver — which agencies currently use en masse, Luckey himself argued on the call. “There’s a number of government agencies that are required by law to buy American-made computers [...] All of those procurements are happening under waivers of those laws,” he said.

We also noticed that Anduril’s press release specifies that its first laptop will be “designed and assembled” in the United States, not necessarily “manufactured” here (though the release does talk about future manufacturing). This was also a sticking point with router maker TP-Link, which would not confirm full manufacturing even after suggesting it in an interview with The Verge.

That’s why we asked Luckey what percent of his first laptop’s components would be made in the US. His reply was not 65 percent or 70 percent. It was “TBD.” You can read the exchange for yourself:

The Verge: When it arrives in 2027, what percent of the components in the BlueChip laptop will be made in the US?

Luckey: TBD. I’m doing my very, very best. It’s gonna be a whole mix of things based on when do certain memory fabs come online? When do certain CPU, GPU fabs come online? There’s a whole— Like, am I able to get a display line here or not? Do I have to do that after launch? I’m being totally open kimono with you here. Those questions are not settled. It is not clear if I will be able. I know the glass is gonna be made in the US, I know the frame is gonna be made in the US around that display. I know that parts of the ribbon component are gonna be made in the U.S. I don’t know if I will have successfully pulled that component in or a dozen other components. So it’s a little up in the air. I’m doing my very, very best. I think most likely the thing that we ship is not going to be 100-percent American. I think it’ll be a blend of US and allies around the world, mostly US, probably a few things from some allies, and then that’ll be in the context of conversations with those allies about bringing those components into production in the United States, and I’m being honest with the government on this too. I mean, I’ve had conversations where I’ve said, listen. I would love to give you guys an American, 100-percent American laptop right off the bat, but that is not the environment the US government has created or US industry has created. I can’t let the perfect be the enemy of the good.

When we followed up by email to ask specifically about 65 percent, the answer was that the laptop prototype is currently at 32 percent and Anduril could not yet commit to 65 percent. Here’s Anduril spokesperson Stephanie Davis’ full reply on Tuesday:

For the prototype today, approximately 32% is U.S.-origin by component procurement cost, and more than 90% is sourced from the United States and allied countries combined.

We’re working to expand domestic sourcing and production ahead of planned availability in 2027, with the goal of eliminating foreign content and ultimately establishing an end-to-end American supply chain.

The final domestic-content percentage at launch remains unsettled, so we can’t confirm above or below 65% today. That’s why we’re asking you to revise the assertion that it will be “well below” that threshold.

Today, Prior writes that Anduril can indeed commit to 65 percent. Here is the most recent statement:

Our previous response to The Verge was incorrect. The BlueChip laptop is indeed being developed to exceed the 65% U.S. domestic-content threshold at launch in 2027. Meeting that threshold is a fundamental objective of the program. The precise domestic-content percentage of the final product is still being determined as we finalize suppliers and work through prototypes, and we look forward to sharing more next year.

Today’s statement is the first time Anduril has confirmed that we, and government agencies, theoretically shouldn’t need to wait until Anduril achieves its full goals to see a laptop that meets the Buy American standard. If everything works out, Anduril’s first laptop in 2027, with Qualcomm, should be that laptop.

We invited Luckey to explain how Anduril would get to 65 percent domestic component production in a follow-up interview, but we were told he’s not currently available. On the Monday call, he told journalists that Anduril already has a US glassmaker lined up for the laptop’s cover glass, is programming FPGAs (field-programmable gate arrays) to serve the purpose of power management chips and other circuits it can’t normally can’t get outside China, and is developing made-in-America batteries as well.

The press release adds that the enclosure is designed “for US machining” and the laptop will use metals “that US industry can produce and machine at scale.”

As for the laptop’s Snapdragon processor, we asked Qualcomm CEO Cristiano Amon about that. “TSMC has a manufacturing facility in Arizona, and that’s where our chips are going to be made,” he told The Verge.

#237. China’s tech darlings are losing their shine. Here’s what’s behind the sell-off

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

China's technology stocks are losing their luster as investors question whether the country's artificial intelligence boom can deliver profits strong enough to justify lofty valuations.

The STAR 50 Index plunged over 40% from its July peak as concerns mount over returns on AI investments. The index tracks 50 of the largest and most liquid companies on Shanghai's technology-focused STAR Market — home to semiconductor, artificial intelligence and other high-tech firms.

Even battery giant CATL, long considered one of China's technology success stories, has come under pressure, falling 37% since the start of the year.

What started as profit-taking after a strong rally has turned into a broader reckoning over whether earnings can support current valuations.

"I think the sell-off primarily started as a valuation correction and some profit taking, but now there is a critical look happening at whether earnings can justify the AI excitement," said Benjamin Cavender of China Market Research Group.

China’s technology rally accelerated after DeepSeek emerged in early 2025, fueling optimism that domestic companies could challenge U.S. counterparts in artificial intelligence.

That optimism has since faded as investors question how quickly companies can turn heavy AI spending into profits.

Tai Hui, Asia-Pacific chief market strategist at JPMorgan Asset Management, said investors want to see how China's tech giants translate their AI spending into earnings, noting that several AI initial public offerings attracted strong initial demand before enthusiasm faded.

However, the challenge extends beyond technology.

China's prolonged property downturn has battered household confidence, while weak consumption continues to weigh on the advertising and e-commerce businesses of major internet platforms.

Vey-Sern Ling, senior equity advisor at Union Bancaire Privée, warned that rising AI investments could further pressure near-term earnings, with longer-term monetization prospects still uncertain.

"Chinese tech stock valuations have reached multi-year lows, which should limit downside, though upside catalysts remain elusive," he said.

The pressure is also evident among China's battery champions.

CATL, a major beneficiary of the global electric-vehicle and energy-storage boom, posted roughly 59% revenue growth in the third quarter, while net profit rose only about 15%.

Competition, shifting product mix and changes to taxes and rebates are squeezing profitability despite its dominant global position.

"CATL's problem is the gap between growth and profitability," Cavender said.

Ling also warned that lower unit profitability could weigh on near-term earnings, even as CATL maintains its global leadership.

The gap between rapid growth and slower profit gains highlights a broader challenge for Chinese technology manufacturers: rapid expansion is not necessarily translating into stronger margins.

Not all Chinese technology stocks face the same pressures.

Brendan Ahern, chief investment officer at KraneShares, noted that Shanghai's STAR Board companies are benefiting from semiconductor spending by Chinese and American tech giants.

Hong Kong-listed stocks, meanwhile, remain more exposed to sluggish domestic consumption, due to their heavier weighting in consumer-facing internet platforms.

Meanwhile, JPMorgan's Hui said China's property downturn remains a fundamental obstacle, with households increasingly parking savings in cash rather than equities.

He nevertheless saw limited downside for broader Chinese benchmarks, citing relatively undemanding valuations and light positioning among international investors.

Some strategists even believe the sell-off may be creating opportunities.

Jason Hsu, chairman and chief investment officer at Rayliant Global Advisors, said the sell-off in Chinese technology stocks has deepened alongside weakness in U.S. equities, as investors grow more skeptical about returns on AI spending.

A recovery in American technology stocks could therefore provide support for Chinese equities.

But cheaper valuations alone may not be enough, according to Cavender, saying that a sustained rebound would require stabilizing earnings, clearer returns on AI spending and stronger domestic demand.

For investors, China's technology sector is no longer about how fast companies can grow, but whether that growth can turn into sustainable profits.

#58. Qatari mediators hand Iran new proposal to end the war

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Updated on: October 28, 2026 / 10:05 PM EDT / CBS News

The Saudi-led coalition fighting Yemen's Houthis said on Thursday it had intercepted a ballistic missile fired by the group at the kingdom's eastern region.

"Air defenses intercepted and destroyed a ballistic missile launched by the terrorist Houthi militia toward the Eastern Province," coalition spokesman Turki al-Malki posted on X.

Saudi Arabia has this month come under sustained attack from the Iran-backed Houthis, including a strike on Riyadh's international airport that was the kingdom's deadliest in years.

The Houthis said this week they had targeted the Ras Tanura refinery in the oil-rich Eastern Province with a ballistic missile.

Tehran has denied having anything to do with attacks by the Houthis after Saudi Arabia accused Iran of having encouraged the group's escalation against the kingdom.

Following an uneasy four-year truce, fighting reignited in Yemen in July, months into the U.S.-Iran conflict that has plunged the wider region into crisis.

Hezbollah chief Naim Qassem said Wednesday the Iran-backed Lebanese movement was not concerned with the results of Israel's election, vowing to continue its "resistance" against the neighbouring country no matter who wins.

With 85% of ballots counted, the right-wing bloc of Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu had a slight lead over the main opposition bloc, led by Gadi Eisenkot, but was still short of a majority after Tuesday's election.

"We do not concern ourselves with who governs Israel, but let it be perfectly clear that it cannot continue its occupation and must leave our land," Qassem said in a written statement.

"The resistance will continue ... and will stand up to the aggression directed against it and against Lebanon, and there is no room to discuss the disarmament" of Hezbollah "as long as the occupation persists and its aggression continues," he added.

The group, which once dominated political life in Lebanon, has been weakened by the two wars it has waged against Israel since 2023.

U.K. Treasury chief John Healey used his first budget Wednesday to offer households and businesses some support with energy bills this winter in the face of what he termed "global economic storms."

He said that his "choice in the budget today is to give families and businesses some breathing space now," as the consequences of the Middle East conflict play out.

"For families, that means higher prices at the pump, higher energy bills and higher mortgages," he said in his statement, one of the main dates of the parliamentary calendar. "For businesses, higher running costs and more expensive loans. And the impact is felt in the public finances, too."

Among the measures announced was a 100-pound ($133) one-off payment this winter for 20 million lower- and middle-income households, proportionately more of whom live in the electoral battlegrounds in northern England.

Other measures include the extension of a cut in the sales tax on electricity bills and the abolition of a charge paid on gas bills.

Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu accused the Arab parties of stealing the election through widespread fraud and called on Shin Bet, Israel's security agency, to investigate.

"We demand from the police and the Shin Bet a thorough investigation into the election theft by the Arab parties and the full prosecution of the criminals," Netanyahu's Likud party shared on his X account, translated from Hebrew. "This is a serious blow to Israeli democracy and the voting rights of Israeli citizens. The law enforcement agencies must act with an iron fist and in an immediate manner."

Netanyahu's party leads in the latest vote counts, but all of the parties will fall short of the majority needed to control the Knesset, Israel's parliament. With 85% of votes counted, Netanyahu's Likud party has 20.8% and Eisenkot's Yashar party has 16.1%, according to official election data.

Local media projected 53 seats for the Likud party, 48 seats for the Yahar and other opposition parties and 19 seats for the Arab-led parties. In order to reach a majority (61 of 120 seats) and pick a prime minister, deals will have to be made.

Amid reports Iran had rejected the latest proposal for peace from Qatari intermediaries, Iranian Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi posted on X that his country continues to review ideas for a diplomatic solution to the eight-month war.

"While our Brave Armed Forces are fully prepared to defend the Homeland, Iran continues to give diplomacy a chance to succeed," Araghchi wrote. "We remain in close and ongoing contact with intermediaries to review ideas that can ensure implementation of our 7-Day Plan in even less than a week."

The seven-day plan was presented to the U.S. shortly after the U.N. General Assembly in September. However, President Trump rejected the idea presented by Iran for a seven-day ceasefire and then phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz.

SMRs are far smaller than traditional large-scale reactors.

"I did not flee Brazil; I left for the sake of Brazil," he said in his first statement since his departure.

The US provided visas to him and his family after Brazil cancelled his passports as part of a criminal investigation. Bolsonaro denies any wrongdoing. On Wednesday, US President Donald Trump told reporters: "We gave him a pass to come into the country".

Newly re-elected President Luiz InÃ¡cio Lula da Silva has criticised Bolsonaro's secretive exit from the country, saying "those who have nothing to hide have nothing to fear".

Watch: Bolsonaro shielded from media by umbrellas at Florida airport

Bolsonaro's arrival in Florida ended days of confusion over his whereabouts, after he was reported to have travelled to Argentina, and then prematurely reported to have already entered the US on Tuesday.

In a statement, Bolsonaro thanked both the presidents of Argentina and the US, and the American public "who share the same values ââas the good people of my own country".

After Bolsonaro won the first round of Brazil's election, Lula won the second round by more than four million votes, which was attributed to significantly higher turnout, and swaying more undecided voters.

Speculation about Bolsonaro's whereabouts was first triggered by a report on an Argentine news site, which claimed that he had crossed the land border between Brazil and Argentina and then boarded a private plane en route to the US.

Brazilian police took the rumours seriously. Early on Tuesday, they checked border crossings but found no evidence that Bolsonaro had left the country, and Argentine officials said they had no reports of him crossing.

For a little while, it seemed like Bolsonaro may just be keeping an uncharacteristically low-profile after being unexpectedly beaten by his left-wing rival in the presidential run-off vote on Sunday, in addition to a criminal investigation that led to orders that his passports be turned into authorities.

But when he failed to surrender his passports in time to meet a 24-hour deadline on Tuesday, Brazilian authorities cancelled the documents and ordered police to make checks at border posts.

Speculation quickly grew that he'd left the country and Bolsonaro's lawyers said they had no idea where he was.

#151. Samsung Electronics shares slid despite record profit in the third-quarter

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

South Korean tech giant Samsung Electronics on Thursday reported third-quarter operating profit that is broadly in line with analysts' expectations, as demand for advanced memory chips used in artificial intelligence applications continued to grow.

Following the announcement, Samsung shares were trading 5% lower.

The company plans to increase sales of advanced memory chips used in AI applications and secure long-term contracts to meet growing demand.

Here are Samsung's third-quarter results compared with LSEG SmartEstimates, which are weighted toward forecasts from analysts who are more consistently accurate:

Samsung's revenue rose 126.88% year-on-year and 13.85% from the previous quarter, while operating profit rose 783.11% from a year earlier.

The company also announced 30 trillion won in cash dividends for the third quarter, equivalent to 4,601 won per common and preferred share.

The company plans to return another 55 trillion to 75 trillion won to shareholders this year through dividends and share buybacks, as the board will finalize the total shareholder returns and specific execution plans at a meeting scheduled for late January 2027.

Samsung said it became the first global technology company to top 100 trillion won in quarterly operating profit, while posting record revenue and profit for a fourth straight quarter.

Its semiconductor business reported 147.1 trillion won in revenue and 106.5 trillion won in operating profit, driven by strong demand for AI-related memory chips.

Furthermore, its memory business also achieved record quarterly revenue and operating profit, supported by a significant ramp-up in HBM4 sales and growing demand for server solid-state drives.

However, Josh Gilbert, lead analyst for APAC at investment platform eToro, appeared unaroused by the numbers, as the results failing to generate much enthusiasm in the market.

"Investors have been so spoilt by the AI trade that a record result is now treated as the bare minimum rather than a reason to celebrate," Gilbert said, adding that Samsung's chip business accounted for nearly all of its operating profit in the third quarter, highlighting its growing reliance on memory chips.

"For all the talk of Samsung being a diversified giant, anyone holding the stock today is really holding a memory maker with a phone business attached," he said, stating that the company's fortunes now depend heavily on memory prices.

Looking ahead, the company expects AI infrastructure investment to accelerate in 2027, with rising demand likely to further tighten memory supply.

Samsung also plans to increase sales of HBM, a type of high-performance memory used in AI chips, along with other memory products used in servers and data storage.

In the third quarter, Samsung Electronics and five of its affiliates had announced a combined $1 billion investment in KKR-backed Helix Digital Infrastructure. Samsung Electronics will invest $500 million, with the remaining amount coming from its affiliates.

The tech giant had also announced a partnership with French AI startup Mistral AI to use AI technologies in its semiconductor operations, including chip design and manufacturing. The company also led Mistral's Series D funding round.

#172. As Waymo expands overseas, it remains a niche cash-burning business

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Waymo is dominant in the U.S. and expanding overseas, but it remains immaterial in terms of revenue to parent Alphabet, which is pouring cash into its growth.

In Alphabet's third-quarter earnings report on Wednesday, the company said its Other Bets segment, which includes Waymo, generated $372 million in revenue, up 8% from a year earlier, and accounting for just 0.3% of total sales.

That revenue comes at a steep price. Operating loss in the Other Bets segment widened to $2.05 billion during the quarter from $1.43 billion a year ago. The unit also houses drug design research business Isomorphic, which is using Google's Gemini 4 Argon AI models.

CEO Sundar Pichai touted Waymo's expansion toward the end of his introductory remarks on the earnings call. The company has a commanding lead in the U.S. robotaxi market, where it faces emerging competition from Amazon's Zoox and Tesla. Waymo said in August that it plans to roll out robotaxi rides in Germany in 2027, marking its third international market and first in the EU.

"Waymo now operates in 15 major U.S. cities and just recently opened up service to the public in San Antonio, San Diego and Tampa with planned expansions into Canada, Germany, Singapore and Spain," Pichai said on the call.

Waymo's steepest competition on the global stage comes from Baidu's Apollo Go, which is based in China.

For years, Alphabet was able to operate Waymo as a side project while its core online ads business racked up tens of billions of dollars in annual profit and threw off mounds of cash. But with the company now investing heavily in artificial intelligence, free cash flow has turned negative and Wall Street has shown signs of concern.

Alphabet reported an earnings and revenue miss on Wednesday, sending the stock down almost 10% in extended trading. The company maintained its full-year capital expenditures forecast at between $195 billion and $205 billion, largely for AI infrastructure, and reported negative free cash flow of over $9 billion for the quarter.

Earlier this month, Waymo announced that it had closed a $5 ‌billion term loan, its first debt financing. In February, the robotaxi business announced that it had raised $16 billion at a $126 billion valuation in a funding round led by Alphabet that included support from Andreessen Horowitz, Fidelity, T. Rowe Price, Sequoia Capital, Kleiner Perkins and others.

Last week Waymo started offering driverless rides to and from the Las Vegas airport, after previously announcing airport service in five other cities, including San Francisco and Phoenix.

"Waymo is on a great trajectory," Pichai said on Wednesday. "We have now driven more than 300 million fully autonomous miles."

Tesla says on its website that driverless vehicles in its Robotaxi network have completed about 3.8 million miles "unsupervised," or without a human driver at the wheel. CEO Elon Musk said on the company's earnings call last week that the Robotaxi business should become a "meaningful" revenue contributor by the second half of 2027.

#358. GitLab Security Incident: Attempted Exploitation of CVE-2026-97229

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On October 20 and 21, an attacker tried to exploit a GitLab vulnerability (CVE-2026-97229) on our instance, a week after the fix was released. The attack came close to working. We have found no evidence that it succeeded, but we cannot rule it out, so we treated the instance as compromised and rebuilt it from scratch. Our checks show that the aports repository, packages, and the October 21 releases were not tampered with.

GitLab is being tested before we open it to the public again. All passwords have been reset, so when it is back you will need to set a new one with “Forgot your password?” on the sign-in page. We will post exactly what you need to do when it reopens. In the meantime: if you used the same password for your gitlab.alpinelinux.org account anywhere else, change it there. We have seen automated login attempts against our GitLab with passwords leaked from other sites.

If you have questions or concerns please reach out to us at infra@alpinelinux.org.

The vulnerability: CVE-2026-97229 is a critical (CVSS 10.0) vulnerability in how GitLab evaluates regular expressions in CI configuration. The underlying bug is in Onigmo, the regular expression engine built into Ruby. GitLab published fixed releases on October 12, but the fix itself is a set of patches to the Ruby that GitLab bundles in its own packages and container images. Upstream Ruby shipped the fix on October 23, in Ruby 3.3.13, 3.4.12 and 4.0.9.

The attack: On October 20 and 21, a newly registered account sent crafted CI configurations to our GitLab instance, which was still running 19.3.4 and did not have the October 12 fix. This crashed GitLab worker processes 8 times. In 4 of those crashes the attacker got partway through the exploit and failed at the next step. The attacker also downloaded our GitLab container image, which suggests they were tuning the exploit to our exact build. We have found no evidence that the attacker ran code on the server, but we cannot rule it out.

Our upgrade didn’t fix the vulnerability: Upgrading to GitLab 19.3.6 on October 22 didn’t fix the vulnerability. The fix isn’t in GitLab’s own code but in a patch to Ruby, which GitLab applies only in its official packages. Our image used standard Ruby, so it didn’t get the patch.

If you run GitLab with your own Ruby (from source or in your own container image) rather than GitLab’s packages, upgrading GitLab is not enough: you also need Ruby 3.3.13, 3.4.12, 4.0.9 or later.

If you use our alpinelinux/gitlab container image, pull alpinelinux/gitlab:19.3.6 again. The image published on October 27 contains Ruby 3.3.13. Any 19.3.6 image you pulled before October 27, and all older tags, are vulnerable.

What could have been exposed if the attack succeeded

Because we cannot rule out code execution, and the evidence we would need to confirm it no longer exists, we took the harder route and rebuilt the GitLab instance from scratch. This removes any foothold a possibly successful exploit could have left behind.

While GitLab is closed, our package mirrors, cgit, and mailing lists/IRC are available as normal.

We have found no sign of tampering in aports or the releases.

Every aports commit added October 20 to 22 arrived through a recorded push by a known developer. We checked this against records the attacker could not reach: a developer’s clone and all 45 builders. Every package built from October 20 to 24, on every architecture, came from commits as published on git.alpinelinux.org. The October 21 releases (3.21.9, 3.22.7, 3.23.7, 3.24.3) were built from those same verified commits.

We ran these checks on October 24, covering commits from October 20 to 22 and packages built up to October 24. Our GitLab stayed vulnerable until we closed it on October 27, so we are extending the same checks to that date.

The bug is in Ruby’s regular expression engine, so any program that compiles regular expressions from untrusted input is exposed, not only GitLab. Alpine 3.22, 3.23, 3.24 and edge ship Ruby 3.4.12, and Alpine 3.21 ships Ruby 3.3.13; both include the fix. Run apk upgrade to get it.

→ Baca artikel asli

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A once-tight friendship between two Victoria’s Secret veterans has become one of the most talked-about celebrity rifts — and it all revolves around legendary NFL quarterback Tom Brady.

Us broke the news in October 2026 that Brady was dating supermodel Candice Swanepoel, days before the modeling legend’s longtime friend Joan Smalls left a shady social media comment seemingly taking aim at the pair’s budding relationship.A source told Us that Smalls previously dated Brady and felt “completely blindsided” after he moved on with Swanepoel.

Scroll breakdown of how the Swanepoel-Smalls fallout unfolded, what each side has said and where things stand now:

Us exclusively broke the news on October 24, 2026, that Brady and Swanepoel had been dating “for a while.”

A second insider later revealedBrady and Swanepoel have been seeing each other since the spring — “several months now.” Neither Brady nor Swanepoel has publicly addressed the relationship, and reps for both initially stayed quiet when Us reached out for comment.

A twist came days later, when a source told Us that Smalls and Brady “were seeing each other on and off for about a year between 2023 and 2024” in a casual fling.

“They were hooking up for a while and still kept in touch afterward,” the source adds.

Smalls and Swanepoel had been best friends for years after coming up together through the modeling world and Victoria’s Secret.

“[Joan was] completely blindsided by the whole thing and felt really betrayed,” a source told Us in October 2026. “To her, it was just the ultimate breaking of girl code. It wasn’t even necessarily about Tom, but more about the fact that Candice was someone she considered such a close friend. Joan had openly talked to Candice about Tom and had confided in Candice about the relationship.”

The inside also claimed that Swanepoel once warned Smalls to be careful dating Brady because his marriage to Gisele Bündchen had recently ended. (Brady and Bündchen, who share two kids, were married from 2009 to 2022.)

Smalls appeared to address the drama in a series of since-deleted social media comments in early October 2026. On a Just Jared Instagram post about the news, she wrote, “Two words: SUMMER HOUSE ! That’ll explain everything” — a reference fans read as a nod to the Bravo love triangle involving Amanda Batula, West Wilson and Ciara Miller.

Also, when asked whether she was still friends with Swanepoel, Smalls replied, “I’m not friends with backstabbers.” In another comment, she wrote, “There is no girl code with some people. And the disrespect and carelessness in how they treat you is so unimaginable.”

Smalls’ comments have since been deleted, with fans also noticing that she and Swanepoel no longer follow each other on social media.

A rep for Swanepoel addressed the swirl in a statement to Us.

“Candice has always been exceptionally private about her personal life, with her focus remaining on raising her children,” her spokesperson said in an October 28, 2026, statement. “She and Joan share a longstanding history and a friendship that Candice will always respect.”

Another source claimed to Us that Swanepoel, who shares two sons with ex-fiancé Hermann Nicoli, has not apologized to Smalls.

The two models crossed paths at the Victoria’s Secret Fashion Show in October 2026, where a source told Us that Smalls “made a point to keep her distance” from Swanepoel.

“While no one really knew what was going on behind the scenes, Joan was pretty upset about the situation,” the source explained.

For now, the two models — once inseparable friends of 15 years — appear to be very much apart.

#184. Upwind raises $400M after walking away from Datadog acquisition offer

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Upwind, a San Francisco startup that uses AI to secure cloud infrastructure, has raised a $400 million Series C round at a $3.8 billion valuation led by Bessemer Venture Partners, with participation from existing backers including Greylock Partners and Craft Ventures.

The round comes nine months after Upwind closed its previous $250 million financing at a $1.5 billion valuation.

The fresh funding, which has been in the works for months, nearly fell apart after Datadog attempted to buy the company, according to a person familiar with Upwind. Datadog is a publicly traded company that sells tools for monitoring how software runs, known as observability. It didn’t offer a premium above the $3.8 billion valuation set by venture capital investors, the person said, so Upwind decided to stay independent.

Four-year-old Upwind has grown rapidly amid rising AI-driven cybersecurity threats. The startup has been adding $20 million to $30 million in annualized recurring revenue (ARR) each quarter and is projected to end the year at roughly $90 million, according to a person familiar with Upwind’s financials. That means that VCs and Datadog valued the startup at more than 40 times its forecast ARR, a common yardstick for pricing software companies. The high multiple is also well below the 80x ARR fetched by Cyera, another fast-growing cybersecurity startup, according to TechCrunch reporting from earlier this year.

Cybersecurity is poised to be the third big wave of AI software that companies will pay for after AI coding tools and customer support, Theory Ventures general partner Tomasz Tunguz recently wrote in his widely read blog.

By walking away from an acquisition offer, Upwind clearly believes that the company will ultimately be worth more than the $3.8 billion that Datadog was ready to pay for it. However, given how few cybersecurity companies have gone public in recent years, an eventual sale remains the most likely outcome for the startup.

Upwind continues to draw acquisition interest, according to a person familiar with the company.

The startup was founded by Amiram Shachar, who sold his previous company, the cloud spend management startup Spot.io, to NetApp for $450 million. Upwind calls its approach to thwarting threats “runtime” security, which means it alerts and remedies attacks and vulnerabilities in active services in real time.

TechCrunch has reached out to both Upwind and Datadog for comment.
