#443. ‘Lord of the Rings,’ ‘Spider-Man’ Digital-Human Pioneer Remington Scott to Head Eros Imagination (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Hollywood technologist who led Gollum’s motion capture on “The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers” and built the first digital humans made with Light Stage technology for “Spider-Man 2” will run Eros Imagination, a new creative technology and experiences business set up by Eros Innovation.

Eros Innovation, an AI company founded by the Lulla family that draws on the film, music and IP library of Eros Media World, announced Remington Scott’s appointment as head of the unit in London. Scott will oversee creative production, the development of specialist talent and international partnerships. Related Stories ‘KPop Demon Hunters’ Gets New Tech Collection at Target, Including Ramen-Themed Chargers, Soda Can Speaker, Pat Pat Light and More 'Project Hail Mary' Composer Daniel Pemberton, 'KPop Demon Hunters' Songwriter EJAE Triumph at World Soundtrack Awards

The new outfit will mix human artists with visual effects, AI, digital-human tools, real-time production and games. Beyond serving Eros’ own businesses, it will go after deals with outside studios, streamers, filmmakers, game developers, musicians, estates and rights holders. It will work next to Eros Brahmand, the group’s film and content studio, and supply technology and production support for films, series, franchises and live immersive shows. Planned services run from AI-assisted filmmaking and post-production to immersive concerts, virtual production and playable game characters.

One of its first projects centers on Mohammed Rafi, the celebrated Indian playback singer. Eros has a signed deal with Rafi’s family, and the plan is an immersive music experience that combines an authorized digital performance with cinematic storytelling, high-end visuals and audience interaction. The project depends on rights and creative approvals. Eros said it is meant to introduce Rafi’s music to younger audiences. It will also give the unit digital-human and production workflows that it could reuse with other authorized artists and properties.

The unit will use Eros Innovation’s in-house Large Cultural Model (LCM) family, multimodal AI that covers language, images, video, voice and music. Eros built the models on its rights-controlled catalog, using about 1.5 trillion rights-cleared cultural tokens. Eros Imagination will team with the company’s India-based AI researchers and engineers to link the models with performance capture and real-time rendering, with humans leading the creative work. A system called Nexus will help choose the AI models and tools for each job, weighing quality, creative control, rights and cost. Eros said filmmakers and artists keep creative control.

The unit will also draw on the AI-powered Creator Studio work Eros Innovation has done with U.K. government bodies and the British creative tech sector.

Eros wants characters, artists and properties made for a film or live show to move into games, interactive stories, social content and new commercial formats where the rights allow it. The unit is also set to build digital identity systems that let artists and estates store, authorize and license digital versions of their faces, voices and performances. The company named consent, authenticity, provenance and IP protection as core principles.

Ridhima Lulla, co-founder and co-president of Eros Innovation, said Eros Brahmand is where the company develops and produces stories, while the new unit “will help build the creative technologies and experiences that allow those stories, characters and artists to move beyond the traditional boundaries of film, music and entertainment. We are not simply bringing together a production team and third-party AI tools; we are building on Eros’s own Large Cultural Models, multimodal intelligence and extensive cultural IP. Remington brings an exceptional combination of filmmaking experience, technical expertise and creative vision, and our Mohammed Rafi partnership provides a meaningful starting point.”

Scott has worked for more than 30 years across film, games and immersive entertainment. He was motion capture director on “Final Fantasy: The Spirits Within” and oversaw digital humans for “Superman Returns,” “Beowulf” and “Watchmen.” “Spider-Man 2” won the Academy Award for visual effects. On the games side, he has worked on “Call of Duty,” “Assassin’s Creed,” “Spider-Man” and “Just Cause.”

He also worked on two Emmy-nominated immersive productions: the Notorious B.I.G. virtual concert “Sky’s the Limit” and a Fox Sports tribute to coach Jimmy Johnson for Super Bowl LIX. His other credits include a Madison Beer virtual concert that won awards and Netflix’s “Altered Carbon” AR experience, which won a Lumiere Award. TheWrap put him on its 2022 Innovators List.

“Artificial intelligence, digital humans, real-time production and immersive technologies are giving filmmakers and creators tools that can fundamentally expand what is possible,” Scott said. “What excites me about Eros Imagination is combining these capabilities with Eros’s filmmaking heritage, extensive intellectual property and cultural intelligence. The Mohammed Rafi experience offers an opportunity to demonstrate how technology can celebrate a legendary artist while preserving the authenticity and emotional connection that made his work timeless.”

Eros said more partnerships and projects for the unit will be unveiled later.

#277. FCC chair wants to let prisons jam illegal cellphones

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The agency will vote next month on relaxing its long-standing opposition to jammers.

The US Federal Communications Commission announced today that it will vote on a new rule that would allow state prisons and jails to use cellphone jammers. The goal of the change would be to block use of illegal cellphones by inmates, since having outside contact could allow them to continue engaging in criminal activity.

The planned vote during the agency's open meeting on November 19 would consider allowing state and local incarceration facilities to opt in to using jammers; use of the tech would not be required. Any tech that interferes with communications signals is illegal for non-federal parties to use. In 2018, the Federal Bureau of Prisons began testing "micro-jamming" in federal prisons as a way to block unauthorized cellphone use by inmates. This move would potentially open up similar tools at the state and local levels, and provide a formal pathway for federal facilities to jam signals as well.

It's a reversal from previous policies where the FCC has not allowed implementing jammers as a method of reducing illegal cellphone use in prisons. Previous concerns about jammers suggested the tech could interfere with emergency and public safety communications, as well as creating new security risks by putting more jamming tools into the wild where they could be exploited by bad actors. The bulletin today notes that if the new rules are adopted, correctional facilities would need to meet some standards, including proving "that the jamming solution does not cause harmful interference outside of the designated geographic areas."

Today's move isn't the first time the FCC has changed its approach toward prisons under the current administration. In July 2025, the department said it would delay implementing a cap on the rates charged for prison phone calls.

The Instagram boss said that worries about AI come down to ‘a fear of being left behind in some way.’﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Asked about AI and the trend of college students booing tech CEOs at graduation ceremonies, Instagram head Adam Mosseri acknowledged in an interview that a lot of people really don’t like AI, and it’s not just young people and creatives. Appearing on the Colin and Samir podcast to talk about the launch of widescreen and long-form video for Instagram, Mosseri said he thinks there’s a trend of “more anxiety and more frustration and more anger about AI,” particularly in creative industries:

It’s not only getting worse, it’s going to continue to get worse. I think we’re going to see more frustration and anger, some of it founded, some of it unfounded, over the next couple of years, and that’s going to make it hard for a lot of people, not just in the industry.

Mosseri claimed Instagram doesn’t rank AI creators differently, but raised the question of ranking quality, saying, “There’s a lot of great AI content out there, there’s also a lot of AI slop. Do we rank AI content as well as we rank non-AI content? Probably not.” Talking about AI slop again after previously bringing it up in a New Year’s slideshow dump, he said he thinks the term AI “slop” is used “too liberally.”

Instagram began cracking down on AI-generated profiles in August, after launching a label for AI accounts earlier this year, but misleading AI content like celebrity deepfakes remains an issue. Earlier this month, Mosseri said Instagram is “actively working out what to do” about AI fakes, but he noted, “I don’t think it’s practical for us to try and ban AI content from the platform.”

Mosseri said Instagram is “now starting to require AI accounts to label themselves, because we think it’s important that people know if what they’re looking at is real or not real.” He also acknowledged there are fears around AI, including from creators:

I think people are concerned about a lot of things. I think they’re concerned about owning their content and models getting trained on their content. I think they’re concerned— there’s people who are concerned about safety. They’re concerned about job displacement, having AI displace whatever it is they do, if you’re a content creator or an engineer or a mathematician. I think there’s concerns about— I mean, fundamentally what I think it comes down to when you really have, like, a heart-to-heart with someone is a fear of being left behind in some way.

#140. Microsoft cuts capex forecast on AI capacity delays

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft reduced its forecast for capital expenditure, a rare move in an industry that mostly continues to splurge more on huge AI investments.

The software giant said capex for the 2026 calendar year will be $170 billion from a previous forecast of $175 billion.

Chief Financial Officer Amy Hood gave the forecast on the company's fiscal first-quarter earnings call on Wednesday.

She said Microsoft was expecting new capacity to arrive from third-parties, but some of these "GPU-as-a-Service contracts" have been delayed until the second half of the fiscal year.

This likely refers to other providers of AI cloud services that use Nvidia GPUs to run their offerings. Microsoft has partnered with a some outside providers to meet rampant AI demand.

Hood's comments could refer to a recent spate of AI data center project delays, which have mostly been caused by a lack of electricity, along with local opposition.

The CFO said Microsoft expects capex to accelerate in the second half of its fiscal year, as the delayed capacity kicks in.

Wall Street has become increasingly concerned the huge investments cloud companies are making to expand AI infrastructure. Microsoft last quarter kept its forecast unchanged, notable as many cloud giants have been steadily increasing their AI spending forecasts.

Overall, Microsoft reported $92.3 billion in revenue, up 19%, for the fiscal first-quarter ending Sept. 30. Azure cloud revenue was up 48% to more than $33 billion, surpassing Wall Street expectations.

"We expect very few people to select this offer because of the growth opportunities here at Microsoft," Hood wrote in an internal memo viewed by Business Insider. Microsoft's headcount slipped 2% during the fiscal first quarter, the company reported.

#96. Hayden Panettiere’s death is ruled accidental and police investigation is closed

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Actor Hayden Panettiere’s death has been ruled an accident and the case is now closed, police in South Carolina announced Wednesday.

“Based on the Medical Examiner’s determination and the findings of the investigation, Hayden Panettiere’s death has been ruled accidental,” Greenville police said, noting the investigation has concluded.

Panettiere, 36, the actor who starred in the shows “Heroes” and “Nashville,” was found unresponsive on Aug. 16 in a South Carolina apartment complex where she was staying.

Last month, the Greenville County Coroner’s Office ruled her death an accident caused by the toxic effects of multiple drugs: fentanyl, 4-ANPP, alprazolam, methocarbamol and quetiapine.

The coroner said there were no signs of trauma that would have contributed to her death. Police also have said no signs of foul play were found during the initial response and investigation.

Panettiere, who had publicly discussed her struggle with addiction, had been in rehab just weeks before, according to the coroner’s report.

Panettiere’s on-again, off-again boyfriend, Brian Hickerson, was at the apartment complex at the time of her death. He showed police a “bag of medication” he said she was taking at the time, according to the police report.

After the coroner’s report was issued, Panettiere’s mother, Lesley Vogel, called her daughter a “fighter” who was “transparent with her addictions and her numerous rehab attempts show her resolve to live a healthy life.”

Vogel also said at the time that she had “never heard Hayden speak of fentanyl.”

The Drug Enforcement Administration was also investigating, a source familiar with the probe previously told NBC News. The agency typically looks into the narcotics involved in a case, how they were obtained and whether any laws were broken in procuring the drugs.

Panettiere entered Hollywood as a child actor and later carved her own path, starring in the films “Remember the Titans,” “Ice Princess” and “Bring It On: All or Nothing.”

In her memoir, “This Is Me: A Reckoning,” she detailed how her life began to spiral after she had a daughter with Ukrainian heavyweight boxing champion Wladimir Klitschko in 2014. She began drinking heavily to cope with postpartum depression and Klitschko’s distance, she wrote in the book.

She checked into rehab and gave Klitschko custody of their child in 2018.

In the book, she also discussed grappling with pain and grief after her brother, Jansen Panettiere, died in 2023 from an undiagnosed heart condition.

If you or someone you know is struggling with a drug or substance abuse problem, call these numbers for help: The Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration’s national helpline is 1-800-662-HELP (1-800-662-4357); the National Drug Helpline is 1-844-289-0879; and the American Addiction Centers’ hotline is (866) 464-3073.

#367. The 2,000-Nit OLED in Apple’s New MacBook Is a Big Deal

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Laptops with OLED screens aren’t particularly special. They’ve been around for a decade. You can currently buy a touchscreen OLED machine for as little as $700, and even the iPad Pro has had an OLED screen since 2024.

So, why did Apple wait over a decade to release its own touchscreen OLED MacBook Pro? The simple answer for the long wait is that the company wanted to do it “the Apple way.” But in this case, “the Apple way” involves somehow cranking up the display to 2,000 nits, solving both the brightness and glare problems that OLED laptops have always suffered from.

Having seen the thing with my own eyes at a media event today in Los Angeles, I can tell you that this screen is the real deal.

Apple is using a tandem OLED panel, the same layer-stacking technology used in the OLED iPad Pro. The real innovation here, however, is that the panel has been optimized to hit a new peak brightness of 2,000 nits, and that’s a result that matters.

That big number is meaningless on its own, but here’s the context. Nits are the standard measure of screen brightness used across the consumer tech industry. A cheap OLED laptop maxes out around 300 nits. A higher-end OLED laptop might get you up to 1,000 nits of brightness—but only in brighter and more colorful High Dynamic Range content like specific streaming apps, YouTube videos, and games. In other words, the vast majority of the time you’re using an OLED, it’ll feel like a dimmer screen than normal. The iPad Pro can get up to 1,600 nits for HDR content, and 1,000 nits for the less bright, less colorful Standard Dynamic Range content. That’s closer.

But this MacBook Pro’s screen is on an entirely different level. It’s a ground-up redesign of the tandem OLED layer arrangement in the iPad Pro, even down to the manufacturing process. And the result is that it can blast your eyes with 2,000 nits of brightness in both SDR and HDR. That means whether you’re looking at some generic wallpaper, a website, or an HDR-compatible movie, you’ll feel that extra brightness. That’s why, I assume, Apple gave it the name PROLED XDR. In a world where the words “OLED” and “HDR” don’t necessarily indicate how good the experience is, more weighty jargon is needed.

As with all tech, bigger numbers don’t always add up to a noticeable difference for the user. But when it comes to HDR performance, we’ve been waiting a long time for OLED to catch up.

HDR, or High Dynamic Range, serves as a measure of color depth, brightness, and contrast—and in the past, this has always involved a trade-off. An OLED screen can turn each individual pixel any color, and it can turn off individual pixels in the display. This allows OLED to have great color control and unbeatable contrast, since when you turn a pixel off, it renders absolutely black.

But OLEDs couldn’t get super bright, and other screen tech like mini-LED could get much brighter, which is why Apple stuck with mini-LED for years, using it on the MacBook Pro and Studio Display XDR. This technology allowed Apple to have the laptops with the best HDR performance out there.

With the 2026 MacBook Pro, that compromise no longer exists. This OLED screen has both the brightness and contrast in its pocket, and you can see it.

An OLED panel has lots of more benefits over conventional backlighting beyond just contrast. Mini-LED struggles with an effect called blooming, where bright parts of images bleed light to the surrounding area, which is where lots of detail is lost in the image. OLED completely fixes that, giving each pixel a much wider range of control (up to completely turning off). As part of the Apple event I attended, we were shown a selection of HDR images with lots of contrast, a number of which had fields of darkness at the edges that made it look like the image simply disappeared into the bezel. Very pretty. We also watched a darker scene in the show Widow’s Bay in HDR, which was an excellent showcase of the range of detail this screen can show.

The new screen helps this MacBook stay exceedingly light, as well. Much of the savings in weight comes from the OLED design, since the screen uses thinner and lighter materials than screens past. Despite the computer using the same aluminum chassis as the previous MacBook Pro, the new 14-inch MacBook Pro weighs just 3 pounds, which is 12 percent lighter than before.

To put the new screen into context, we were also shown a comparison between Apple’s OLED and the OLED of a high-end competitive Windows laptop. The difference was striking, especially in off-axis viewing. At its worst, there were visible rainbow effects on the Windows machine’s screen.

There’s a catch, though. While Apple’s screen can get up to 2,000 nits, it’s normally capped at 1,000 nits. When the laptop’s built-in adaptive brightness sensor thinks you need a boost, the screen’s brightens increases up to 2,000 nits. So the brightness only hits the high end when you’re outside or working under really harsh lighting conditions. The previous-gen MacBook Pros also did this, but to a lesser degree. With this machine, you’re getting 400 more nits of SDR brightness right out of the box, with the ability to crank it up to twice as much.

Glare is another issue with conventional, low-brightness OLED laptops that the MacBook Pro improves. When deeper blacks are rendered on the screen, it often results in harsher reflections. Sometimes it can get so bad, it feels like you’re looking into a mirror. The brightness alone on the MacBook Pro goes a long way toward overpowering glare and reflections, but Apple is also using what it claims is a “state-of-the-art” anti-reflective coating. Almost every laptop screen uses some kind anti-reflective coating, so one can hope that Apple’s “state-of-the-art” coating is at least on par with those, but I can attest that this is the very best I’ve seen.

The ultimate solution to cutting glare and reflections is still the option for a nano-texture display, where the anti-reflective pattern is actually etched into the screen itself rather than layered on top. That’s available as an upgrade on both the 14-inch and 16-inch models.

The other major innovation happening with the MacBook Pro’s screen is the introduction of touch. Like OLED itself, this is a feature we’ve seen on many other laptops for well over a decade. I came in hoping Apple would swing for the fences here, but its use of touch interaction feels much more modest. (Let us quickly forget the failure of the Touch Bar from 2016.)

The implementation itself is impressive. Interacting with elements on the screen feels every bit as fluid as it does on an iPad. The screen also supports many of the familiar trackpad gestures, such as pinch to zoom or a three-fingered swipe to switch Spaces.

The most natural feeling touch optimizations have been lifted directly from iPadOS. If you tap in the top left window controls, they zoom in to let you select more precisely. You can even grab a window and flick it to the side to quickly get into Split View. My favorite optimization is the ability to tap, hold, and slide down to select an item within the Menu Bar. Very clever. And yet, I was still hoping for a stronger commitment on the software side. You can’t use an Apple Pencil on this screen, and nothing in the hardware of the MacBook Pro has been adjusted to make touching the screen feel any more ergonomic.

The bigger issue is with third-party apps, though. While touch-to-click will work on all apps automatically, it’s up to developers to do any more than that. Even the third-party apps we were shown during demos had varying degrees of touch optimization.

The 3D modeling application ZBrush was a good example. While certain aspects were touch-enabled, none of the controls or menus had the useful optimizations that Apple’s first-party apps had. Hopefully this touch option will become more widely adopted, but I can foresee a future in which these fingers-first interactions are mostly forgotten outside of occasionally scrolling down a webpage with your thumb. And let me be clear: All of this is more than what Microsoft has ever done to optimize Windows 11 for touch. (And Microsoft even has Windows 2-in-1 tablets to support!)

With the 2026 MacBook Pro, Apple is doubling down on what’s working well for it: leading the way in high-end hardware. There’s plenty more testing to do on the MacBook Pro in the coming weeks, but this OLED screen already looks like the one the entire industry will be chasing for years to come.

#194. Apple’s touchscreen MacBook Pro pushes laptop pricing to a new extreme

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Apple has officially entered the touchscreen MacBook Pro era, and apparently, it wants to drain your wallet while it’s at it.

The new touchscreen MacBook Pro starts at $2,899, which is already a hefty price tag in this economy. But if you want the absolute top-of-the-line model with every possible upgrade, prepare to cough up around $14,549.

As MacRumors first reported, Apple’s configurator prices the fully loaded 14-inch model at $14,249 and the 16-inch version at $14,549. Both come equipped with Apple’s top-tier M5 Max chip, featuring an 18-core CPU and 40-core GPU.

You can blame the ongoing memory shortage and AI’s seemingly endless appetite for hardware for some of the pricing pressure. Moving to a 4TB SSD costs an additional $1,000, while upgrading to 8TB adds $3,000. Want the new 16TB option? That’ll set you back another $7,000.

Another $2,000 gets you 128GB of memory, while nano-texture glass adds $150.

For some perspective, the previous 16-inch MacBook Pro topped out at $10,149. The new model raises that ceiling by a whopping $4,400.

Absurd prices aside, the launch marks a significant shift for Apple, nearly a decade after it first experimented with the Touch Bar. The company eventually abandoned that feature, but now it’s ready to embrace a touchscreen MacBook Pro.

Preorders open October 30, with availability beginning November 6.

#468. Everything to Know About Hayden Panettiere’s Death

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Actress Hayden Panettiere was found unresponsive at a residence in Greenville, South Carolina, on August 16, 2026, and pronounced dead at the scene. She was 36.

According to a statement from the Greenville Police Department, officers, “accompanied by EMS personnel, responded to a report of an unresponsive female” at approximately 1:50 p.m. “Upon arrival, medical assistance was administered; however, the individual, later identified as Hayden Panettiere, was pronounced deceased at the scene.”

The Greenville County Coroner’s Office said Panettiere had been “temporarily staying” at the residence when an acquaintance placed the 911 call.

“EMS personnel initiated advanced cardiac life support measures; however, resuscitation efforts were unsuccessful, and the individual was pronounced deceased at 2:32 p.m.,” the office said.

Her death was officially ruled an accident one month later. Police, however, wouldn’t conclude the investigation until October 2026.

Scroll below for everything to know so far:

A police report obtained by Us revealed that Panettiere’s ex Brian Hickerson and his brother, Zach Hickerson, were at the apartment complex where the actress’ body was found. Per the documents, Brian provided a “bag of medication” that Panettiere was “currently on.” According to the incident report, life-saving measures were performed on Panettiere for “an extended amount of time” before she was pronounced dead.

“After EMS officially declared Hayden deceased, I escorted Zach and Brian out of the apartment,” the police report stated. “I observed Zach to be very emotional while EMS were working on Hayden. Brian did not become emotional until EMS declared her officially deceased.”

Days later, on August 19, 2026, two Greenville Police Department cars were seen outside a South Carolina property owned by the Hickersons’ grandmother.

“Greenville Police officers responded to a call for a welfare check at a home belonging to Brian Hickerson’s family on Wednesday morning,” a police statement shared with Us read. “The call is unrelated to the Hayden Panettiere death investigation and did not require an incident report.”

Brian broke his silence over his on-off girlfriend’s death in a statement released to Us through his lawyer Sloan Ellis in August.

“It is important to allow that investigation to proceed,” Brian’s lawyer told Us. “As has been publicly reported, police confirmed there were no signs of foul play. Out of respect for Hayden’s loved ones and the ongoing investigation, there will be no further comment at this time.”

Panettiere’s autopsy was completed on August 17, 2026.

“At autopsy no signs of trauma were discovered that would have contributed to the death,” the coroner’s office said in a statement, adding that the cause and manner of death “remain pending further investigation and the completion of additional studies.”

Greenville police also noted the “preliminary investigation has not indicated any signs of foul play or suspicious circumstances.” Panettiere’s body was released to a mortuary chosen by her family on August 18, 2026.

Dispatch audio obtained by outlets referenced an “overdose” and “cardiac arrest.” First responders attempted advanced cardiac life support, which, in addition to CPR, included special medicines, breathing tubes and heart rhythm tracking.

Panettiere’s cause of death was officially revealed on September 22, 2026.

“An autopsy was completed by a Board Certified Forensic Pathologist. At autopsy no signs of trauma were discovered that would have contributed to the death,” read an official statement from the Greenville County Coroner’s Office.

According to the statement, Panettiere’s cause of death was ruled as “toxic effects of fentanyl, 4-ANPP, Alprazolam, Methocarbamol, and Quetiapine.” The manner of her death was determined to be an accident.

A rep for the actress told Us, “The investigation is still ongoing and the case is complex.”

The New York Times reported on August 20, 2026, that the Drug Enforcement Administration (DEA) was looking into the circumstances surrounding Panettiere’s death.

A source familiar with the situation claimed to the outlet that Panettiere died after an apparent overdose led to cardiac arrest.

Us reached out to the DEA for comment at the time.

Hayden’s father, Skip Panettiere, announced his daughter’s death in a statement shared via the actress’ rep.

“It is with profound sadness that we share the tragic passing of our beloved Hayden,” Skip said at the time. “She was an incredible light and a force of nature who brought immeasurable love and joy to all who knew her — and to the millions who watched her onscreen.”

Hayden was the daughter of Skip and his ex-wife, Lesley Vogel, who also publicly mourned the Heroes alum. (Skip and Vogel also shared son Jansen, who died in 2023 at age 28 after an undiagnosed heart condition.)

“I think it becomes very difficult to be true to yourself and I think Hayden sadly lost her way and I wish it were different,” Vogel told NBC News, in part, at the time. “I wish she had stayed true to herself because she had many incredible attributes.”

In addition to her parents, Hayden is survived by her 11-year-old daughter, Kaya, whom she shared with ex Wladimir Klitschko. The pair got engaged in 2013 and welcomed Kaya in 2014 before separating in 2018. Hayden later gave up custody of Kaya as she battled postpartum depression and addiction — a decision she once called “absolutely one of the worst days of my life.”

“Our family is going through a time of profound shock and grief. The announcement of Hayden’s tragic death saddens me deeply. She left this world far too soon,” Klitschko wrote via Instagram, mourning his ex. “To our daughter Kaya, I will always speak of her mother with respect and make sure she remembers the person she was.”

Hayden took a hiatus from acting in 2018 while struggling with postpartum depression and alcohol and opioid addiction. She entered treatment for a third time in 2020, spending eight months in rehab, and detailed her struggles in her 2026 memoir, This Is Me: A Reckoning.

Her final public appearance was on May 26, 2026, in West Hollywood at an event celebrating the publication of her memoir.

More than two months after Panettiere’s shocking death, police shared a major update on the investigation into the events surrounding her reported overdose.

“Based on the medical examiner’s determination and the findings of the investigation, Hayden Panettiere’s death has been ruled accidental,” the Greenville Police Department in South Carolina said in a statement to Us on October 28, 2026. “The Greenville Police Department has concluded its investigation.”

If you or someone you know is struggling with substance abuse, contact the SAMHSA National Helpline at 1-800-662-HELP (4357).

#272. Hands-on with Apple's first touchscreen laptop: Touch is totally optional

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple announced its first touchscreen laptop models on Wednesday at an event in Los Angeles.

These new MacBooks, called MacBook Pro with ProLED XDR Display, are still Apple MacBooks. They run MacOS, not iPadOS or iOS, the iPhone's operating system.

Here's the good news for anyone who is worried about the new user interface: You don't have to use it. Touch is entirely optional.

CNBC briefly tested both the 14-inch and 16-inch touchscreen MacBooks at the event.

Users can still primarily or entirely use the device's trackpad and keyboard, and there are few visual updates. MacOS still looks largely the same as it used to.

There have been a few user interface updates. For example, the red button on the top left of a window expands when you touch it to make it easier to click. Apple says that third-party developers like Microsoft and Adobe can and will update their apps to expand buttons and other touch targets to be larger. I think that touch will be particularly useful to scroll through websites or documents while lying down.

But even software that hasn't been explicitly updated for the new computer will support touch. Apple says that behind the scenes, its software converts every touch tap into a traditional click. So any button that can be clicked can also be touched.

Using the laptop with your finger feels good, but it won't change the way most people use their laptops. It could be useful for iPhone app developers, who can now test the way their apps work on their Mac. An accessibility feature allows users to pinch to zoom on any part of the user interface.

Apple said on Wednesday that it had used an oleophobic coating on its screens to resist fingerprints. It appears to work. I didn't notice marks on the screen when I used the touch features.

Apple is also, in addition to the touch feature, marketing these laptops as having a superior display, thanks to its new tandem OLED technology that offers deeper blacks and higher contrast.

While photos and other art looked great on the screen, it's not a night-and-day difference from past Apple laptops. A lot of the improvements are aimed at creators who need accurate colors for photo and video editing.

Instead of the notch at the top of previous MacBooks, where Apple hides its cameras, the new MacBooks have a hole-punch cutout in the display, like recent iPhones. Apple's Dynamic Island feature — new to the MacBook — expands to show live activities and Siri.

Apple made a big deal about the laptop's new hinge, which is meant to keep the screen from shaking when you touch it. For example, if you tap the screen on a video call, your camera won't shake as much. But the hinge feels largely the same as Apple's previous MacBook Pros.

There is one new feature that is immediately noticeable and tells people you bought the latest, greatest, and most expensive Apple laptop. (The smaller 14-inch model starts at $2,899 and the bigger one costs at least $3,399.) The Apple logo on the lid now lights up when the computer is on. It's the first Apple laptop that supports it since the 2017 MacBook Air.

The light-up logo drew a cheer from analysts and influencers in attendance when it was announced on Wednesday.

#52. Girl seriously injured in wolf attack at German wildlife park

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The park keeps Arctic wolves, like those pictured, and grey wolves (file image)

An eight-year-old girl has been seriously injured in a wolf attack at a German wildlife park.

She was inside an enclosure at the JohannismÃ¼hle park in Brandenburg, south of Berlin, with a staff member and two other children when the attack happened, police said.

They said in a statement that the "precise circumstances of the incident are the subject of an ongoing police investigation".

The girl was taken to hospital by helicopter after the incident on Wednesday. No one else was injured.

The park, in the town of Baruth/Mark, has been closed temporarily and is set to reopen on 2 November, according to its website, which did not give further details.

The park keeps grey and arctic wolves, along with other animals including the brown bear and lynx.

The girl was injured by at least one animal and suffered head injuries, according to German newspaper Bild.

It reported that the incident happened before the park opened at 10:00 local time (09:00 GMT) and that the girl could have been taking part in a special visit.

JohannismÃ¼hle offers small group tours to allow visitors to spend time with some animals, with its site mentioning feeding wolves or petting a llama.

The incident comes after a number of other animal attacks at zoos and wildlife parks in Europe.

A keeper at a Dutch zoo died this week after being mauled by a leopard, and a man was killed by a tiger at Yorkshire Wildlife Park earlier this month.

#203. Microsoft just shared Azure’s actual revenue in dollars: $33.2 billion

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Microsoft has never disclosed Azure’s quarterly revenue in dollars. It has typically only shared a growth percentage or described revenue dollar milestones in vague ways. But on Wednesday, when it posted another record-breaking quarter, it reported that Azure brought in a healthy $33.2 billion in revenue for the July-September quarter, up 48% from a year earlier.

The disclosure lets investors line up Azure’s performance more directly against Amazon Web Services and Google Cloud, whose parent companies already report cloud revenue in dollars every quarter.

Alphabet, which also reported earnings on Wednesday, said Google Cloud brought in $30.7 billion in the same quarter, putting Azure slightly ahead. But Google Cloud, which also includes Google’s Workspace subscriptions, roughly doubled its revenue from a year earlier, growing about twice as fast as Azure, according to TechCrunch’s calculations.

Azure, where businesses rent computing power to run applications, SaaS, and to build and run AI models, is now Microsoft’s largest and fastest-growing product line by the company’s own figures. It accounted for roughly 36% of Microsoft’s $92.3 billion in quarterly revenue.

Azure’s revenue was also about $850 million more than the $32.3 billion analysts expected. Azure’s 48% growth rate also topped the 44% to 45% that Microsoft CFO Amy Hood forecast on the company’s July earnings call.

Azure sits inside Agents and Infra, one of two new business segments Microsoft introduced this quarter. Microsoft previously reported three segments, Productivity and Business Processes, Intelligent Cloud, and More Personal Computing. The new Agents and Infra segment, which groups Azure with Microsoft 365’s cloud business, brought in $76.8 billion, up 25% from a year earlier.

Amazon reports its earnings on Thursday. Analysts also expect its cloud, Amazon Web Services, to grow. It’s been the largest cloud since the beginning of time and no one expects Microsoft to best it right now. But with all of them reporting actual dollars, the race is on.

#268. A hacker stole more than 40,000 DHS employee records

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The names, job titles, phone numbers, and emails of thousands of ICE, CBP, and other DHS employees were compromised in a massive hack.﻿

The names, job titles, phone numbers, and emails of thousands of ICE, CBP, and other DHS employees were compromised in a massive hack.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

A hacker stole a trove of personal information from the Department of Homeland Security (DHS). The breach exposes the names, job titles, phone numbers, email addresses, and office locations of more than 41,000 employees, including those in US Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) and Customs and Border Protection (CBP), according to TechCrunch and Wired. The data also linked the agencies to specific surveillance tools.

As reported by TechCrunch, a hacker known as “Department of Peace” gained access to the data through Okta, an identity and access management service used by the DHS. Around 21,000 records pertain to CBP employees, and nearly 7,000 belong to staff at ICE. The data also includes records from the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA), US Citizenship and Immigration Services (USCIS), and Homeland Security Investigations (HSI).

The DHS has worked to conceal the identities of CBP and ICE agents, who have carried out anti-immigration raids around the US, while also tracking down online critics. “They have our information, so it’s only fair that we have theirs,” the “Department of Peace” hacker said, according to TechCrunch. This leak comes just weeks after hackers stole data from the FBI on its employees and job applicants.

The leaked DHS information was reportedly given to cybersecurity researcher maia arson crimew, who shared it with Distributed Denial of Secrets (DDoSecrets), a nonprofit organization that’s now hosting the leaked “Icepick” dataset. The leak also reportedly includes information about DHS groups that have access to tools that can be used for surveillance, such as the facial recognition app Mobile Fortify and PenLink, which can collect and analyze data from phones and online services.

The DHS didn’t immediately respond to The Verge’s request for comment.

#211. Google expands AI Search ads globally as core ad growth slows

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Google is bringing ads in its AI-powered Search experiences to international markets, betting that generative AI can open up new revenue opportunities even as growth in its core search advertising business slows.

The announcement came Wednesday during Alphabet’s third-quarter earnings call, as the company reported $127.6 billion in revenue, up 25% year over year. But Search advertising growth continued to decelerate, and Alphabet shares fell 8% following the results.

Google Search revenue grew 12% year over year in the third quarter, down from 17% growth in the second quarter and 19% in the first quarter. The slowdown puts added pressure on Google to turn its expanding AI features into a meaningful source of advertising revenue.

“We are now scaling high-quality ads within AI overviews in AI mode to more users and queries,” Philipp Schindler, Google’s chief business officer, said during the call with investors. “This includes taking our monetization footprint global for the first time. Today, we’re announcing the expansion of ads in AI mode into international markets alongside multi-turn ads and AI mode in the U.S.”

Google says early results suggest the formats are working. Click-through rates for sponsored product listings in AI Mode are already comparable to those for organic listings, according to Schindler. The company’s user research also indicates that people find the ads relevant.

The move builds on Google’s May announcement of new Gemini-powered ad formats, including conversational discovery ads that respond to users’ questions and sponsored recommendations integrated into AI-generated answers.

Google CEO Sundar Pichai also said that Search queries grew at their fastest year-over-year rate in five years, though he did not disclose the exact growth rate. He credited AI features with changing how people use Search, including prompting longer and more complex queries.

Queries in AI Mode are three times longer on average than traditional searches, Pichai said, while one in five AI Mode searches globally involves voice, images, or documents. Google has also surpassed 50 billion visual searches per month.

“[Search] is bringing the benefits of AI to more people than any other product in the world,” Pichai told investors. “People are using our AI features more, searching in entirely new ways. Their queries are longer, personalized, and multimodal.”

Alphabet’s Google Services division, which includes Search, generated $96.3 billion in revenue, up 11% year over year. Google Cloud revenue surged 103% to $30.7 billion.

The results follow a busy quarter for Google, including the release of Gemini 4 Argon, the growth of the Gemini app to 1 billion monthly users, and the launch of its Pixel 11 lineup.

#2. High-speed train crash kills at least 68 people in Spain

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: 'Chaotic scenes' at site of Spain's deadly train crash

A high-speed train crash has killed at least 68 people and injured dozens more in central Spain.

It occurred when a train travelling from Madrid to Seville barrelled into another train - which was stationary on the tracks - on Wednesday morning. The force saw several carriages fall down an embankment.

The transport minister said a computer failure followed by human error caused the crash, which is one of the worst in Europe's modern history, and Spain's deadliest train disaster in 13 years.

Survivors described harrowing scenes of passengers being thrown into the air, trapped in the wreckage and smashing windows to get out.

The crash happened just after 09:00 (08:00 GMT) around 140km (87 miles) south of Madrid, between the municipalities of Mora and Orgaz.

The stationary train, run by private rail operator Iryo, had 147 passengers on board, and had stopped on the tracks because of a computer failure, Spain's Transport Minister Ãscar Puente said.

He explained that when there is a system failure, a 24-hour control centre in Madrid takes over, but it had made an "inexplicable" decision to instruct the trains to move again, without any speed restrictions.

Iryo said its train was "struck from behind" by another train, run by Spain's state-owned railway company, Renfe, which was carrying 397 passengers.

The force of the crash was "brutal", said 62-year-old passenger Salvador Arriza who was sitting in the Renfe-operated train.

Passengers started breaking the windows to get out, he told Reuters, adding that "some people couldn't move because they were trapped amongst the seats."

One of the carriages caught fire, and passengers described devastating scenes of trapped people crying and bleeding, with some buried under debris.

Pictures show that a long piece of railway track had speared through one of the carriages.

The force of the crash saw several carriage fall down an embankment

Tangui Binier, 22, was on the Iryo train and told the BBC that "suitcases started flying and people were screaming" when the train finally came to a halt.

There was a lot of smoke and confusion, he said, and described seeing the train "completely flipped over" when he stepped outside.

"It was hard to witness", Binier said, adding that his father carried a girl who had a broken hip out of the wreckage.

He also described seeing dead bodies and missing limbs: "I don't know if I'll ever recover."

Some passengers said the piece of train track that speared through a carriage may have slowed the train down

Spanish journalist Antonio JimÃ©nez Barca, who works for El Pais newspaper, was walking to the Renfe train's cafeteria when the crash happened.

"I felt like I was being pushed from behind, like I was being thrown to the ground," he described.

"I hit my head horribly... I was covered in blood. There were people on top of me. One person had an injured arm, and another had a head injury."

Another passenger, 46-year-old SebastiÃ¡n Herrero, told El Mundo newspaper that he saw "many people with broken noses, broken arms."

"It was a terrible, terrible impact," he said, adding that he was "in the air for about four or five seconds," which felt like a lifetime.

Many of the passengers said they were in shock, and one sobbed during an interview with a local television station as he described a woman dying in his arms.

"She was very cold and she was all alone," he said, to which the show's presenter also started to cry.

Dozens of ambulances rushed to the scene, and a helicopter evacuated those with the most serious injuries.

Spain's Civil Guard said it would need DNA samples from relatives to identify some victims.

"The whole of Spain is grieving", Prime Minister Pedro SÃ¡nchez said during a visit to the crash site. "Lives have been shattered, families have been torn apart, the pain is immense."

Most political parties in Spain cancelled their campaign events scheduled for Wednesday.

The exception was the far-right party Vox. Its leader, Santiago Abascal, said his party would not take part in what he described as attempts to "use mourning to silence criticism" of the country's rail infrastructure.

Spain was already reeling from a high-speed rail disaster near CÃ³rdoba in January that killed 46 people. It happened on the same rail line, but much further south.

The prime minister said it was "unacceptable" for Spain to have two serious train crashes in one year, and vowed to "get to the bottom of this" and determine if there was any negligence.

"Spain has the right to know what is happening with its infrastructure," said Alberto NÃºÃ±ez FeijÃ³o, leader of the the main opposition People's Party.

"If there have been failures in the security system, they will be addressed with the utmost firmness."

Spanish trade union CSIF has demanded the transport minister resign, accusing him of "scapegoating" a traffic manager, by blaming human error in the control room, instead of "taking responsibility".

Wednesday's crash is the worst the country has seen since 2013, when when 80 people died on the outskirts of Santiago de Compostela in north-west Spain.

#220. Microsoft recorded $10 billion in revenue from OpenAI

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Microsoft generated $10 billion in revenue from commercial arrangements with OpenAI, including revenue-sharing payments, in the quarter ended September 30, the company said in a regulatory filing on Wednesday. That represents nearly 11% of Microsoft’s total quarterly revenue of $92.3 billion, highlighting the enormous financial ties that remain between the two companies even as they increasingly compete for the same customers.

(Microsoft’s financial year begins in July, making the quarter ended September 30, 2026 the first quarter of its fiscal 2027.)

For the full 2026 fiscal year, revenue from OpenAI hit $24.1 billion. At its latest quarterly pace, Microsoft would pull in $40 billion annually from OpenAI-related arrangements.

Microsoft also reported $7.2 billion in outstanding receivables from OpenAI at the end of September, up from $6 billion at the end of June. That money represents amounts OpenAI owes for previous transactions, not additional revenue.

It’s not clear how much of Microsoft’s revenue from OpenAI came from revenue-sharing payments or Azure cloud computing services. TechCrunch has reached out for more information. In November 2025, TechCrunch reported, citing leaked documents, that Microsoft had received $865.8 million in net revenue-sharing payments from OpenAI during the first nine months of that year.

OpenAI reportedly shares 20% of its revenue with Microsoft as part of a previous deal in which the software giant invested more than $13 billion in the AI startup. (Neither the AI lab nor Microsoft have publicly confirmed this percentage.) As part of an April 2026 agreement, OpenAI is on the hook to continue sharing revenue with Microsoft through 2030, subject to a cap, even as OpenAI is allowed to work with competing cloud providers.

The companies began loosening the ties of their once-exclusive partnership in October 2025, when Microsoft relinquished its hold on OpenAI to give the startup more freedom to partner with other cloud providers. In exchange, OpenAI committed to buying an additional $250 billion in Azure services as part of an October 2025 agreement.

Microsoft’s ownership stake in OpenAI has also declined slightly from about 25% in June to 24% in September. That continues a gradual decline from roughly 27% following the October 2025 recapitalization. Microsoft did not specify what caused the dilution.

#279. Akai is embracing vibe-coded mods with an open-source MPC

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The MPC Sample Key isn’t even formally announced yet, but Akai is already showing off what the modding community is cooking up through vibe coding. Akai has decided to embrace the explosion of hackers and custom firmware developers by giving them early access to the device, which will have open-source firmware.

It will likely run some version of the same software found on the MPC Sample, but with tweaks to accommodate the traditional keyboard, pitch, and mod wheels. Unlike the higher-end MPC Key 37, however, the Sample Key doesn’t have pads. Instead, you play back samples with the keys.

In the short Instagram Reels posted last night and today, Akai shows a number of vibe-coded mods that add synths, including Native Instruments Massive, to the device, as well as virtual analog and FM synths. One is even shown running a completely custom OS, with “several different synths in it.”

According to the clips, the same “community” features will come to the regular MPC Sample, which is also shown running a few vibe-coded synths built by Claude. That could mean Akai plans to open-source the entire MPC Sample platform.

CEO Jack O’Donnell told The Verge that inMusic brands, especially Akai, would be looking to embrace the modding community by seeding early devices to developers. These efforts will also include open-sourcing some software and providing an SDK.

#230. Microsoft hits 10 quarters of record-breaking revenue in a row

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Disrupt doors open Oct. 13. Get your pass and bring someone with you at 50% off. REGISTER NOW.

Whatever the future of AI might be, the current situation is still very good for Microsoft. The tech giant reported yet another quarterly revenue record on Wednesday: $92.3 billion, a 19% increase over the year-ago quarter.

Microsoft has now reported record-breaking revenue for 10 quarters in a row.

This is profitable revenue, too. Microsoft reported that net income reached $36.6 billion, an increase of 32% on a GAAP basis, over the year-ago quarter.

The record-breaking income was largely driven on the strength of Microsoft Cloud, which reached $63.9 billion in revenue this quarter, an increase of 30% over the year-ago quarter, Microsoft said.

Microsoft has reorganized its business reporting into two basic segments: 1) Agents and Infra and 2) Devices and Consumer. On the flip side, revenue in Devices and Consumer was $15.5 billion, decreased of 3%, with Windows OEM and devices revenue falling 22% from the year-ago quarter.

True, not all of Microsoft’s healthy cloud revenue can be attributed to AI. Microsoft 365 accounted for nearly $28 billion. But Azure brought in the most, at $33.2 billion. And again, not all of that is necessarily AI-related. Enterprises run all sorts of the traditional software and SaaS applications on Azure.

Still, a good chunk of the revenue was from AI, because Microsoft also reported $10 billion in revenue from OpenAI. Despite branching out to AWS, Oracle, and others, OpenAI still runs a lot of its services on Microsoft’s cloud. (Anthropic runs on Azure as well.) Microsoft also said its “commercial remaining performance obligation increased 73% to $677 billion.” That represents future revenue committed under existing contracts of the AI labs.

But, like all the big cloud providers, Microsoft is also spending big to prepare to deliver on those commitments. Capital expenditures topped $45.2 billion, another all-time quarterly record.

#12. Bloodied, dazed and angry - how passengers' rail journey turned to disaster in Spain

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Survivor describes being onboard when trains collided

It was approaching 08:30 (07:30 GMT) on a normal Wednesday. Two high-speed trains were travelling south from the Spanish capital, Madrid, to Seville.

One, run by state-owned operator Renfe had 397 passengers on board. Ahead of it on the same line was a train of Iryo private operator with 147 passengers. A reported issue with the automatic signalling system - a computer failure - had forced all trains to halt on the affected stretch.

Then the impossible happened, Spain's transport minister said, as the Renfe train was ordered to proceed in "error".

It crashed into the back of the other train between municipalities Mora and Orgaz, about 140km (87 miles) south of Madrid.

Watch: "People flew off their seats," says passenger

Some of the passengers were asleep, some had already started their working day on laptops - and others scrolled through their apps on their phones.

The journey was interrupted with an announcement that there was congestion on the tracks. This lasted a few minutes - for some, a bit longer for others.

Tangui Binier, 22, was in the Iryo train, the front carriage, number eight. His father was in number seven.

Their train was one of six to have been ordered to stop at 08:30. A few minutes later, "the train started speeding", Binier told the BBC, "then it came to a sudden halt".

Baffled, he looked out of the window and saw "wires on the left side come down, and then there being just a lot of smoke behind us."

Spanish journalist Roberto GÃ³mez was dozing on the high-speed Renfe train which had stopped more than 25km (16 miles) away from Binier's.

It was a brief stop. A few minutes later, the train began to move again, he explained, and then the crash happened.

He did not know that at 08:36 his train had been ordered to proceed with the journey and some 17 minutes later the "truly unbelievable" happened, Spain's Transport Minister Ãscar Puente said.

"The impact was terrible," GÃ³mez told Onda Cero radio. "You can imagine, the carriage is completely destroyed. All the suitcases are all over the wrecked carriage."

GÃ³mez suffered only minor injuries but described another passenger falling on top of him and sustaining a deep gash on his head.

"We were able to help people who had suffered severe blows to their chest and ribs," he said.

Rafael Barcelo was in one of the worst-affected carriages. A bruise on his forehead makes him one of the lucky survivors. He was having a coffee at the time of the crash and "felt a huge impact".

"We were all thrown into the air," he told Reuters news agency at the site of the crash.

"I am reasonably OK, my friend is quite worse."

"I was travelling in coach seven, which is the one with more victims and the one most destroyed. I at least counted eight victims in that coach," he said, visibly shocked.

Rafael Barcelo: "I counted eight victims in that coach"

Even in the carriages farthest away from the impact on the Renfe train "everything was completely destroyed - the seats were detached, and most of the people who were travelling facing the same direction as the train were thrown into the air", according to Salvador Arriza.

"It was something brutal," he said, describing how some people "were trapped between the seats".

Once out, he saw how bad the situation was.

"What caught my attention is that in one of the coaches there was a piece of train track that went through the roof of the coach, and I think that might have helped to stop or reduce the inertia of the train."

Salvador Arriza says a long piece of track that speared through the carriage roof may have slowed the train down

Bloodied, dazed and angry - how passengers' rail journey turned to disaster in Spain

High-speed train crash kills at least 68 people in Spain

SebastiÃ¡n Herrero was travelling in the train which was hit from the rear and the "terrible, terrible impact" threw him on to the seats in front.

"After we fell, there were many people with broken noses, broken arms... We started seeing smoke everywhere."

The crash near Urda is Spain's worst rail disaster since 2013

Once the initial impact was over, passengers in Binier's carriage debated whether it was safe to leave because of the electrical wires strewn outside the train.

Some were worried about the risk of another train colliding and decided to exit.

"We did not know what had happened until we took a step outside, and there there was evidence of what had happened.

"We saw the other train behind ours and we saw our train completely flipped over and the back side full of debris," he told the BBC.

"When I got out of the train I was in complete shock, complete awe. There were people in danger, it was very hard to see, there was dead bodies, there was missing limbs. "

"I don't know if that is something I will ever recover from."

But the adrenaline kicked in, he said, and those like him who had not suffered any injuries went to help.

Among them was his dad, who he explained was "able to carry a girl to safety, with a broken hip".

It was not long before local people arrived, followed by the police and ambulance services of the Guardia Civil.

"I'm really one of the most fortunate people and the luckiest person. I feel like just I'm so fortunate and I see other people who are not so fortunate, and I see them very close up. Sometimes you just ask yourself 'Why me?'," he said.

Spain was already reeling from the Adamuz high-speed rail disaster outside CÃ³rdoba in January, in which 46 people died.

It happened on the same high-speed rail line, but much further south. So Wednesday's accident has raised many questions for the authorities.

"It isn't possible that our train was stopped for five or 10 minutes and that the other train wasn't told to stop," one of them said.

"How can this happen with the technology we have?," they added. "It's not normal. We weren't told anything. The person who should have been told something was the driver of the other train. We wouldn't have cared as long as the other train had been warned. I don't understand this."

The Founders Edition is a limited run of the Form Studio 1, available at launch while supplies last. It comes in signature Graphite or limited-edition Aqua, Violet, or Teal, and each printer displays a Founders Edition tag in its interface. Founders Edition perks include your first bottle of Alpha Resin for $29, a limited-edition Form Studio 1 plushie, and 25% off Form Now orders, up to $100 (US only).

The Form Studio 1 is a desktop resin 3D printer for creators, designers, hobbyists, and studios who want professional-quality prints. Popular uses include tabletop miniatures and figurines, cosplay props, product design prototypes, art and sculpture, custom gifts, and small-batch parts for side businesses.

The Form Studio 1 is an MSLA printer built on Low Force Display (LFD) technology. Its backlight unit uses 28 high-powered LEDs and collimating lenses to cure resin quickly and evenly across the entire build platform.

Most prints finish in under 2 hours (based on the 80th percentile print height of 53 mm at 100 µm layer height), and print time stays the same whether you print one part or a full build platform. With Alpha Fast Resin, the Form Studio 1 prints at up to 130 mm/hour.

XY dimensional tolerances are ±0.5% for 1–30 mm features, ±0.2% for 31–80 mm features, and ±0.03% for 81–150 mm features, with a lower limit of ±0.08 mm. A 35 µm pixel size and pre-tuned anti-aliasing deliver sharp detail, smooth surfaces, and parts that fit together as designed.

In internal testing, the Form Studio 1 has shown print success comparable to Form 4, which achieved a 98.7% print success rate in independent testing. Full reliability testing for the Form Studio 1 is underway.

The system is designed to be highly intuitive. Most users can learn the full printing workflow in about 10 minutes.

PreForm, Formlabs’ free print preparation software, automatically orients models, generates supports, and slices your files, so you can optimize your layout and send jobs straight to the printer.

Form Studio 1 is not compatible with third-party slicers or print preparation software.

The Form Studio 1 supports six Alpha Resins developed specifically for it: Alpha Grey, Alpha White, Alpha Black, Alpha Clear, Alpha Fast, and Alpha Model. It also supports Tough 1500 Resin, Flexible 80A Resin, and True Cast Resin. Every supported material comes with validated print settings tested on the Form Studio 1, so you get reliable results without calibration or trial and error.

Most other Formlabs resins are also compatible. These dispense automatically from the cartridge, but you'll need to tune your own settings and use the Form Studio 1 Resin Tank Pro. Print performance may be lower, and mechanical properties may differ from published values.

You can also print third-party 405 nm resins using Open Material Mode. Automatic dispensing is disabled in this mode, so you'll pour resin manually and tune your own settings.

The Form Studio 1 Resin Tank uses a film engineered for Alpha Resins, which gives faster prints and a better finish on supported surfaces. Some materials, including Tough 1500 Resin V2 and most third-party resins, contain ingredients that damage this film, so they require the Form Studio 1 Resin Tank Pro.

Never print these materials in the Form Studio 1 Resin Tank. The film can degrade quickly and leak resin, which can damage your printer and leave a mess to clean up.

Resin tanks are consumables, and their lifespan depends mainly on the number of layers printed and accumulated wear. With proper maintenance and Formlabs materials, Form Studio 1 resin tanks are designed to last more than 75,000 layers. The tank film can’t be replaced on either the Form Studio 1 Resin Tank or the Resin Tank Pro.

After printing, wash parts in IPA or Formlabs Resin Washing Solution to remove uncured resin, then UV-cure them to reach final mechanical properties. Form Studio Wash+Cure, or Form Wash and Form Cure, automate both steps.

Yes. A built-in camera lets you watch active prints, view time-lapses, and review your print history from the Form Mobile app or Formlabs Dashboard.

Yes, in three ways: cover it with the included camera cap (in the accessories box or print your own from the preloaded print jobs), turn it off in the printer settings (also shown during onboarding), or unplug the camera cable to disconnect it completely. The camera and time-lapse are separate settings, so you can turn off time-lapse while keeping the camera on.

#73. Death of US actress Hayden Panettiere ruled accidental, ending local police probe

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Police in South Carolina, where Hayden Panettiere died in August, say they have concluded their investigation into her death

The death of US actress Hayden Panettiere has been ruled accidental, ending a local probe into her death, police in South Carolina have said.

The Heroes and Nashville star died in August from the toxic effects of fentanyl, Xanax and several other drugs in her system, at the age of 36.

Citing the medical examiner's report last month that found Panettiere died from a drug overdose, the Greenville Police Department said it had "concluded its investigation" into her death.

The US Drug Enforcement Agency (DEA) reportedly joined a local police probe. It is unclear if that investigation has also concluded. The BBC has contacted the DEA for comment.

"Based on the Medical Examiner's determination and the findings of the investigation, Hayden Panettiere's death has been ruled accidental," police said in a statement.

Authorities, including the DEA, were reportedly investigating the circumstances of Panettiere's death, according to the BBC's US media partner CBS News.

Watch: Hayden Panettiere on her career and growing up in the spotlight

A spokesperson for Greenville Police Department could not comment on what the DEA was investigating when contacted by the BBC.

Panettiere, who had spoken openly about her struggles with substance abuse, was found unresponsive in a South Carolina residence on 16 August.

Drugs at their fingertips, intense pressure: Why child stars fall into addiction

In pictures: Her life and career, from child actor to Scream star

Last month, the Greenville County Coroner's Office confirmed the former child star had died from an accidental drug overdose that included fentanyl - a synthetic opioid that has killed hundreds of thousands of Americans over the past five years alone.

The coroner also found methocarbamol - a prescription muscle relaxant - and quetiapine - an anti-psychotic medication used to treat certain mental health conditions.

The coroner's office had said crews treated a woman in "cardiac arrest" and her autopsy showed "no signs of trauma" contributing to her death.

Panettiere's ex-boyfriend, Brian Hickerson - who has admitted to abusing her - and his brother Zack were with her when emergency services arrived, according to a police report from the day she died.

#289. Readwise is taking on Kindle and Kobo for ebook supremacy

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Taking on Kindle is hard. Beating Goodreads might be really easy.﻿

Taking on Kindle is hard. Beating Goodreads might be really easy.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

For the last several years, Readwise has been building tools for power readers. There’s the eponymous product, Readwise, which stores and categorizes your Kindle highlights to help you remember what you’ve read. There’s Readwise Reader, an app for reading newsletters, PDFs, and web articles you’ve saved for later. And now there’s Readwise Books, a full-fledged platform for buying, reading, and talking about books.

“Strava for reading” wasn’t the original goal of Readwise Books, which has been part of the Readwise plan since the beginning. “Literally, Dan and I talked about this when we first met,” Homsi says. “We feel like Kindle could be so much better. We really didn’t think it would take nine-plus years to be able to get to this point.” But it turns out, Doyon adds, that for all their ideas about innovation in books, “no one sits around wanting a better Kindle.” But there is one thing they do wish for: a better Goodreads. “Everybody hates Goodreads.”

In a sense, then, the plan for Readwise Books is to take on Kindle by first taking on Goodreads. The Readwise Books app offers a nice way to track books you want to read, are reading, and have read; it even syncs with your Kindle account to let you track books you’ve bought from Amazon by taking pictures of your starting and ending pages. You can rate and review books and see what others have said. It’s a little Strava, a little Letterboxd, and a lot nicer to look at than Goodreads.

The long-term goal, of course, is to get people to stop buying Kindle books and start buying from Readwise. Homsi and Doyon have spent the last several years working to reach deals with all five of the major US publishers in order to have a competitive library of books available. That meant endless negotiations, security reviews, DRM debates, and more — both cofounders seem exhausted just talking about the process. But eventually, by several small miracles, they got the deals done. And since those deals govern the price of books, and many things about how they’re sold and presented, they bring Readwise to a nearly even playing field almost immediately.

It all took a lot longer than expected, but Doyon and Homsi think their timing might actually be pretty good. Digital book reading continues to grow, particularly among young people; a huge amount of that reading happens on phones and tablets. Meanwhile, a new crop of E Ink devices like the tiny Xteink and the Boox Palma line have expanded the universe of available hardware for a company like Readwise.

Not long ago, Doyon says, they would have had no choice but to build an e-reader of their own. (And he says they still might.) Now, they can just make sure it runs on an E Ink-friendly flavor of Android.

After a couple of days using Readwise Books, I can say confidently I already like it better than practically all of its competition. It’s a much nicer-looking app than anything you’ll get from Kindle or Kobo; it has lots of ways to customize how books look; and at least for now, I’m finding the tracking features compel me to read more instead of annoying me. (Homsi says some early versions of the gamification features were substantially more annoying.) I’ve found a few bugs, and the half-integration with Kindle is a little confusing in spots, but it’s already a really nice place to read.

Of course, I, like millions of other people, already own a Kindle and lots of Kindle books. Switching ebook platforms is hard, and Doyon says he’s not counting on everyone to do it right away. He says he’s hopeful Readwise can persuade existing users to switch, in order to sync their notes and progress more seamlessly. He’s also hoping that the advent of AI, and the ways in which AI reliance may rot our brains, will get a lot of people back into the idea of reading more deeply again. And that a bunch of those people might be looking for a nicer, more social place to do so.

#260. CoreWeave and other neocloud stocks drop after Microsoft capex comes in below expectations

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Shares of neoclouds like CoreWeave and Nebius dropped in extended trading as Microsoft's lower-than-expected capital expenditures figure points to infrastructure partners taking longer to deliver data center capacity.

Microsoft on Wednesday reported $45.2 billion worth of capex and finance leases during the quarter, up about 30% from a year earlier, but below the $50.7 billion average analyst estimate, according to StreetAccount.

Microsoft is particularly reliant on neoclouds for capacity as it pours money into its artificial intelligence infrastructure. Microsoft inked a nearly $20 billion deal with Nebius at the tail end of last year for compute through the 2030s. It's also a top customer for CoreWeave.

Those companies and their peers have experienced a rocky few weeks on the market as rising interest rates led to concerns that future debt raises will be too expensive and lenders will start retreating.

In its earnings report, Microsoft said Azure revenue jumped 48% from a year earlier, far outpacing the increase in capex.

"Growth was higher than capital expenditures driven by timing differences between receipt of goods and payment and a lower mix of finance leases," Microsoft said its earnings deck.

CoreWeave and Nebius slid almost 5% after Microsoft's report. IREN fell about 3%.

Alphabet on Wednesday also reported capex numbers that fell short of estimates. The Google parent's capex totaled $51.36 billion, while analysts expected $55.09 billion.

SpaceX CEO Elon Musk, whose company rents AI infrastructure to Anthropic and Google, predicted over the weekend that more than 200 neocloud companies will collapse, posting on X that "debt markets have almost entirely shut off access to money for any company that doesn't have substantial revenue independent of AI data centers."

And last Monday, analysts at Gartner shared with CNBC its prediction that, by 2030, 80% of neocloud companies will either be absorbed or completely cease operations.

After a steep plunge this month, the neoclouds had been enjoying a modest recovery this week, with CoreWeave up 18% in the past three trading days to pace the gains. Analysts at Wolfe Research wrote in a report on Tuesday that they see the stock rebounding into the mid-$70s. It closed Wednesday at $63.93.

The infrastructure story gained some strength earlier on Wednesday as Oracle issued a strong long-term revenue forecast and said it will return to being free cash flow positive in the second half of next fiscal year. The stock jumped 11% in regular trading before retreating 3% following Microsoft's report.

—CNBC's Jordan Novet contributed to this report.
